- `fix(voice): the sentence handed to Kokoro as its positional argument always begins with exactly one space, whatever it starts with, so the six Unicode dashes a narrow Windows argv folds to - cannot be read as an engine flag.` README 3.1, ADR 0069 amended (council full r1 robustness-1): the deny-list on an ASCII dash is withdrawn; tests in tests/channels/test_voice_engines.py cover U+2010, U+2011, U+2012, U+2015, U+2212, U+FF0D, U+2013, U+2014, U+FE63 and an ASCII -- sentence.
- `fix(voice): a bare yes, ok, do it or no answers only the newest card this socket presented; a pending card it never presented is answered only by its own option id or label, and only when exactly one such card fits, otherwise the utterance goes on as a message; the inbox is read newest first.` README 3.1, ADR 0073 (council full r1 robustness-3): the one-inbox-card fresh-socket case is unchanged; tests in tests/channels/test_voice_gateway.py.
- `fix(voice): a tool_result's tier and ms are accepted only as non-negative JSON integers and anything else gets turn.error validator_failed with the socket open; an exception while handling a frame is logged by type and closes with 1011, a framing error closes with 1002 and an oversize frame with 1009, a normal end still 1000.` README 3.1, ADR 0019 (council full r1 robustness-2, robustness-6): the 1011 door in server.py now fires and nothing is logged with a frame's content; tests in tests/channels/test_voice_gateway.py.
- `fix(voice): one utterance is at most 4,194,304 bytes of PCM, two minutes, the cap /voice/transcribe already has; the frame that would cross it is not fed, the utterance is dropped unbilled with one turn.error validator_failed naming the limit in seconds, later frames are ignored until the next start, and the socket stays open.` README 3.1, ADR 0072 (council full r1 robustness-5, economics-1; hard failure unbounded_foreign_decode): BufferedTranscriber refuses past the same ceiling; tests in tests/channels/test_voice_gateway.py.
- `fix(playbooks): the bench sends the simulated person to another portal only for a phrase shaped like a request, the base verb switch, go, move, head, flip or change then to, back to, over to or into, or open, then the portal, so "the claim goes through Stallwick's form" no longer sends her to Stallwick.` Bench tooling sweep item 3: it misrouted follow-ups in medicare-plan-review, name-change-everywhere, oversize-permits and counterfeit-takedowns; the earliest request still wins and the cap of three follow-ups is unchanged; tests in tests/proving/test_playbooks.py.
- `fix(playbooks): the prose audit checks a total in her closing words against the dollars the cards filed and against the value found, and agrees when it equals either within a cent; with no filed figure it checks the value found alone.` Bench tooling sweep item 2: a re-bench of lease-operator-escrow no longer reads as her words disagreeing with the record, and a stated total on registration-only cards (mlc-unmatched-royalties) is now audited; said_usd, record_usd and agrees keep their meaning; tests in tests/proving/test_playbooks.py.
- `fix(playbooks): bench.json now records score.neutral, the count of cards judged neutral, which summary_of used to drop.` Bench tooling sweep item 1; tests in tests/proving/test_playbooks.py.
- `feat(memory): a fact reaches the always tier only through a core.write_fact card the user approved with every_turn: true; the file records it as its last frontmatter key so a rebuild keeps it, and each always-tier line enters the system prompt as one quoted, neutralised line.` README §2 invariants 1, 2 and 4, ADR 0071 (amends ADR 0003; council-lite r1 provenance-backed-memory value-1): the field is optional and boolean, core.write_fact stays GATED with its provenance validator unchanged, the flag fixes importance at 4, a file without it rebuilds to the same rows as before, ALWAYS_LINE_CAP is 400 and the block keeps its (showing N of M) footer; tests in tests/test_wiring.py, tests/core/test_brain_store.py, tests/core/test_brain_reconcile.py, tests/core/test_recall.py and tests/core/test_catalog.py.
- `fix(daemon, contracts, catalog): POST /manifest refuses a posted connector manifest and an app_id no session can key, and puts the origin and pin back if a step after its merge raises; a params_schema of the wrong shape is refused whole and one already in a catalog renders as unreadable and refuses every call; a manifest is capped at 96 tools and 24 parameters a tool, and the capabilities block at BUDGETS['capabilities'] characters, cutting foreign names in block order and announcing the cut with (showing N of M).` README 3.2, 3.3, 4, ADR 0070 (council injection-resistance r1 robustness-1, robustness-2, robustness-3, economics-1): measured maxima 41 tools (Ledgerbox with the nine hands) and 8 parameters (Hirelane search_candidates); core entries always shown, a cut name keeps its class at the gate; 200 origins of hands render 11,977 characters, (showing 162 of 1803); tests in tests/daemon/test_manifest.py, tests/daemon/test_hands_manifest.py, tests/contracts/test_manifest.py, tests/core/test_catalog.py and tests/core/test_validators.py.
- `fix(brain): a rebuild names a file that is not valid UTF-8, and a fact whose expires_at is a list or an integer past 64 bits, instead of aborting on it.` README §2 invariant 1, ADR 0003 (council-lite r1 robustness-1, the remainder after the confidence fix): the messages are `<rel>: not UTF-8` and `<rel>: expires_at is not a single value`, the expires_at check runs beside the confidence check so the file leaves no node, FTS, provenance or fact row, and every value that binds today binds unchanged; tests in tests/core/test_brain_reconcile.py.
- `fix(brain): a rebuild names a fact or procedural file whose confidence is not a finite number instead of aborting on it, and names a file with no sources instead of passing over it.` README §2 invariant 1, ADR 0003 (council-lite r1 robustness-1 and robustness-3): the refused file is checked before any insert and leaves no row, the message is `<rel>: confidence <value> is not a number`, a confidence of 0 is kept, an absent one is the default, `ReconcileStats.unsourced` lists the sourced-less files that stay indexed and does not change `ok`; tests in tests/core/test_brain_reconcile.py.
- `fix(voice): the sentence handed to Kokoro as its positional argument never begins with a dash, so a reply shaped by a page cannot be read as an engine flag such as --output-filename.` README 3.1, ADR 0069 (council-lite r1 robustness-1): one leading space on a dash-led sentence, any other sentence byte for byte; tests in tests/channels/test_voice_engines.py.
- `fix(prompt): the pending-decisions block is wrapped in the turn's nonce-tagged fence and marked untrusted, so a pending card's params, proposed by the model after reading a page, cannot read as an instruction.` README 3.2, ADR 0006, ADR 0068 (council-lite r1 robustness-2): PENDING_LINE_CAP and the daemon route are unchanged; tests in tests/core/test_prompt.py.
- `fix(catalog): a page's or connector's tool description, parameter names and enum values enter the capabilities block as one capped JSON-quoted line, with a fixed sentence that they are data, so a description cannot forge a heading or an OP: line and the block's hash still holds.` README 3.2, ADR 0006, ADR 0068 (council-lite r1 robustness-1): descriptions cut at 240 characters, enum values at 12 of 40 characters, core entries unchanged; tests in tests/core/test_catalog.py.
- `fix(hands): every capture the shell files is followed by a captures sweep at the default cap, and startHands sweeps once, so the declared 64 MiB cap holds on the shipped path.` README 3.5, ADR 0066 (council r2 economics-1): the sweep is not awaited and a failure is only logged, so a capture's id and why never change; a capture that failed, no shell and a page that is not the web file no row and sweep nothing; the Rust command is unchanged and evicts the oldest-written rows first; tests in hands.test.ts.
- `fix(webmcp): an exchange reads the focused tab once, and the manifest, every request's gated lists and the routing of the calls it releases all come from that one snapshot, in run and in voice.` ADR 0067 (README 3.3, 3.5; closes council r2 robustness-1 and robustness-5): a refresh between the publish and the request, in either order or across a continuation, no longer moves the session's app off `gated_origins`, and a released call goes to the executor the gate classified; tests in run.test.ts and voice.test.ts.
- `feat(playbooks): rank ranks all 100 playbooks by ADR 0056 and playbooks/ranking.json is committed: 99 ranked, osha-300-log unranked as short, lien-desk the best use at 11.98, then clinic-denials and estate-settlement; rank --check exits 1 when the file drifts.` M4: the method of ADR 0056 with its two amendments and ADR 0064, computed from the committed playbook.json and bench.json only, no model call; the nine rows of the ADR's worked table are pinned by test to 0.01.
- `feat(playbooks): a civic nonprofit's records requests on Friday 9 October 2026 get three appeals inside 90 days, one for a denial that came only by mail, an overdue request chased, a narrowing, a city's search fee disputed and two new requests, while a late appeal, requests inside 20 working days, a city appeal, a fee and a records service are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds.` ADR 0057 row 91 (B9): 5 U.S.C. 552(a)(6), (a)(4)(A), (a)(7)(B), 28 CFR 16.6 and 16.8, OPM's 2026 holidays and the California Public Records Act (Gov. Code 7922.530, 7922.535, 7923.000), as they stand on Friday 9 October 2026 (FOIA has no administrative appeal of a late answer, so the roster's "appeal the late answers" is a message about a late request and an appeal only of a determination; the 90 days are the statute's floor and the invented agencies' letters state them; the Act has no administrative appeal); value 500 dollars a year, nine items at a 1 dollar count weight, 150 minutes, 12 times a year; Filerecords, Agencydesk, Binderwell and Postwren, all invented; 9 of 9, $9.00 of $9.00, 15 of 15 traps, 0 false, 2.2 min, $1.38; Mailnest and Docshelf renamed before the bench.
- `feat(playbooks): a small firm triages its solicitations on Friday 9 October 2026: six are kept, one because a mailed amendment changed its set-aside, and four questions go in before their deadlines, while set-asides it does not hold, a size standard it exceeds, a plan over the 50 percent limit, a cancelled and a closed notice, a local-only county bid, two late questions and two paid services are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds.` ADR 0057 row 90 (B9): FAR Part 19 as agencies apply the RFO model text by class deviation, 13 CFR 121.104, 121.106, 121.201 (SBA's table effective 17 March 2023) and 125.6, as they stand on Friday 9 October 2026; employees are averaged over 24 months and SBA's August 2026 proposed size standards are not in force; economics 0 dollars a year with a 1 dollar count weight per act, a price avoided and not money recovered; Noticewick, Bidmoor, Contractdesk and Postwren, all invented; 10 of 10, $10.00 of $10.00, 12 of 12 traps, 0 false, 2.4 min, $1.92; Bidline, Procurenet and Mailnest renamed before the bench.
- `feat(playbooks): a kitchen nonprofit's restricted funds are released on Friday 9 October as their terms are met: a cooler grant in service, a gift whose date a mailed amendment moved up, 6,300 dollars of a meals gift and 9,400 and 5,700 dollars of two conditional grants, with two earlier releases reported to their funders, while a van, a gift for 2028, a later date, a grant 104 of 150 earned, a reserve, an endowment and a consultant are left alone, across six portals, benched to exceeds.` ADR 0057 row 89 (B9): FASB ASC 958 (the text of ASU 2018-08 and PwC Viewpoint's copies of ASU 2016-14 and 2018-08 and guide 6.7, because the Codification needs a login), as it stands on Friday 9 October 2026 (a condition is a barrier plus a right of return; a match, costs and milestones are recognised to the extent met and a whole-or-nothing grant is not; restrictions expire with the date or the purpose, equipment when placed in service; a board designation is no restriction and an endowment's corpus is never released); Grantpoint, Binderwell, Ledgerwren, Donorsheaf, Fundergate and Postwren, all invented; 7 of 7, $74,600.00 of $74,600.00 (dollars released or reported, an exposure and not money recovered), 10 of 10 traps, 0 false, 2.8 min, $1.70; Donorbook, Ledgerline, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench.
- `feat(playbooks): a grain farm's 2026 acreage report is filed before 15 July, as farmed and not as insured or as last year: a flooded corn plan replanted to soybeans, a 60 percent share lease, prevented planting, a crop after wheat, while rented-out, custom, sold, idle and future ground, wheat on file and a paid filing service are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds.` ADR 0057 row 88 (B9): 7 CFR Part 718 Subpart B (eCFR, 10 July 2026), the FSA-578 and farmers.gov; the final reporting date 15 July 2026 taken from FSA's 2025 Iowa notice and 2026 notices of other states because no Iowa notice for 2026 was found; Farmgate, Acrelog, Fieldmark, Postwren all invented; 7 of 7, $132.65 of $132.65 (18.95 dollars a field line, a price avoided and not money recovered), 8 of 8 traps, 0 false, 2.3 min, $1.69; Mailnest renamed Postwren before the bench.
- `feat(playbooks): a school district's FCC Form 471s for funding year 2026 are filed on Friday 13 February, inside the window, with the lowest eligible bid for four services: Internet, managed Wi-Fi, switches whose best bid came only by mail, and a fiber link, while the vendor that drafted a Form 470, a Form 470 posted 11 days earlier, bus Wi-Fi, Chromebooks, a service already filed, a consultant's share and a gift card are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 87 (B9): 47 CFR 54.503 and 54.511 (eCFR), USAC's pages on the funding year 2026 Form 471 window (21 January to 1 April 2026) and the 28-day waiting period, the funding year 2026 Eligible Services List (DA 25-1069, 17 December 2025) and the Category Two notice DA 25-471 (201.57 dollars a student), as they stand on Friday 13 February 2026 (the rule says four weeks, which USAC counts as 28 days with the day certified as day one; the price of the eligible services is the primary factor; a provider that prepares the Form 470 makes the process not fair and open; no rule or USAC guidance in force bans a consultant's percentage fee, and the FCC only proposed a ban in June 2026); Rateline, Ledgerwren, Binderwell and Postwren, all invented; 4 of 4, $43,600.00 of $43,600.00, 12 of 12 traps, 0 false, 1.7 min, $1.20; Rateline EPC, Brightwave Networks, Ledgerline, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a flooded household's three FEMA appeals are filed by 19 October: 7,300 dollars of the house's repair and 2,150 of its contents that the insurer's limits left unpaid, and 2,950 for the flooded car, while a furnace the insurer paid, a lake cabin, landscaping, an approved rental, a neighbour's estimate, a decision 60 days past, a claim service and a contractor are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 86 (B9): 44 CFR 206.113, 206.115, 206.117, 206.119 and 206.191 as Cornell's copy of the eCFR held them (ecfr.gov and the Federal Register refused every fetch), the Individual Assistance rule in force since 22 March 2024 as 206.119 now reads, and the 2026 maximum of 44,800 dollars for housing assistance and for other needs assistance from a state emergency agency's summary of the Federal Register notice of 29 September 2026, as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the appeal is due 60 days after FEMA notifies the applicant, which the world makes the date on the decision letter; the Stafford Act, FEMA's Individual Assistance Program and Policy Guide and the 2024 rule itself were not opened); a declared disaster, DR-9017, invented; Reliefmoor, Hearthguard, Lenswick photos and Postwren, all invented; 3 of 3, $12,400.00 of $12,400.00, 8 of 8 traps, 0 false, 1.7 min, $1.61; Reliefline, Snapshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a disabled worker's earnings are reported to Social Security in the month they were earned, June to September, 1,447, 1,408, 1,274 and 1,320 dollars, with a second employer whose stubs came only by mail, while three months already on file, October, her husband's job, a work expense, a paid advocate and a text are left alone, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 85 (B9): 20 CFR 404.1592 and 404.1592a (eCFR and Cornell's copy), 404.1588 (Cornell's copy), POMS DI 10505.005 (revised 10 January 2025), DI 13010.035 and DI 13010.060, and Social Security's trial work period fact sheet (the 2026 amount, 1,210 dollars), as they stand on 9 October 2026 (wages count in the month earned; no work incentive is applied when trial work months are counted; the duty to report is 'promptly' with no day of the month; ssa.gov refused every fetch of its figures page, so the 2026 substantial gainful activity amounts were read in secondary sources only and no target uses them); Benefitline, Payroll Harbor and Postwren, all invented; 5 of 5, $4,000.00 of $4,000.00, 8 of 8 traps, 0 false, 1.1 min, $0.84; Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a laid-off father's and a pay-cut mother's changes go to the college's aid office with proof and an estimated 2026 income by its 16 October deadline, while a 2024 cut, a seasonal job, a brother's other college, a tuition rise, a premium, a dependency override and an appeal service are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 83 (B9): HEA 479A (20 U.S.C. 1087tt, Cornell's copy: (a) case-by-case adjustments on documentation and no policy of denying all requests, (b) a condition that differentiates the student and a change in income, (c) a dependency override for unusual circumstances, which a job loss is not) and the 2026-27 FSA Handbook's Application and Verification Guide, chapters 2 and 5 (last modified 17 August 2026: the decision is final and cannot be appealed to the Department; 2026-27 uses 2024 income), as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's list of documents for a job loss, a separation letter, a determination and final pay, is not in the Handbook, so the proof list and the counting of severance and benefits are the invented college's, and the caveat says so); Aidcenter, Fernhollow Unemployment Desk, Payroll Harbor and Postwren, all invented; 2 of 2, $4,000.00 of $4,000.00, 10 of 10 traps, 0 false, 1.9 min, $1.51; Joblink State and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a student's FAFSA verification is answered by 16 October with the documents the college names: a notarized ID, a five-person family statement, the 2024 return transcript, her non-filer statement and two W-2s, one in a 2025 letter, and the family size fixed, while old forms, a paid service and the IRS's figures are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 82 (B9): 34 CFR 668.51 to 668.61 as the eCFR held them on 8 October 2026 (the 2016 text), the Department's 2026-27 notice (90 FR 54316) as the 2026-27 FSA Handbook's Application and Verification Guide and Dear Colleague Letter GEN-25-10 list it (the notice's item charts are images), and the 2026-27 Pell maximum, 7,395 dollars (GEN-26-01), as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's signed or notarized statement is gone with the statement of educational purpose, and only identity still asks for a notary in person; the Department's 2026-27 Pell date is not yet published, so the world's date is the college's own); Aidcenter, AidPath, Taxmoor Federal Account and Postwren, all invented; 7 of 7, $7,000.00 of $7,000.00, 16 of 16 traps, 0 false, 1.8 min, $1.37; Taxline Federal and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a distributor's background-check rejections are taken in the order the FCRA sets: four pre-adverse notices with the report and the summary of rights, final notices for three whose own wait has ended and a namesake's dispute sent on, while a skills test, a hire, a missing authorization and a corrected report are left alone, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 81 (B8): 15 U.S.C. 1681b(b)(2) and (b)(3)(A), 1681m(a), 1681i, 1681j(b) and 1681n as in Cornell's copies, the FTC staff's Weisberg letter of 27 June 1997 (five business days "appears reasonable", not binding: the statute names no number of days, so the world's own policy is the basis and no target rests on a statutory number) and the CFPB's summary of rights (Appendix K to 12 CFR Part 1022, its current version, the eCFR text not read), as they stand on 9 October 2026; no state or city fair-chance rule applied; Applyrook ATS, Veritarn Screening and Postwren, all invented; 8 of 8, $8,000.00 of $8,000.00, 24 of 24 traps, 0 false, 1.4 min, $0.86; Talentry ATS, Clearcheck and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

VIII sec. 24-a and Labor Code 201.021 and 201.043, Washington RCW 50.04.080 to .120, DOL UIPL 20-04 and the 2023 Comparison of State Unemployment Insurance Laws, as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's "before the first payroll" is no applied state's rule: the date is the payroll firm's invented rule, and Minnesota words its own 50 percent test); Crewmarrow HR, Postwren, Payroll Harbor, Taxline State and Rollmark State, all invented (the last two stand in for state agencies' portals); 5 of 5, $995.00 of $995.00, 14 of 14 traps, 0 false, 2.0 min, $1.23; Staffnest, Mailnest and Revenuedesk renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a machine shop's 2025 OSHA injury log is closed under Part 1904: seven cases entered with their days, one a privacy case and one made recordable by a declined prescription, the summary sent to the plant's top official to certify and the 300A and 300 and 301 data to submit, while first aid, a parking-lot crash, the flu, a sandwich and repeats are left alone, across four portals, benched to short` — ADR 0057 row 79 (B8): 29 CFR Part 1904 (1904.1 to 1904.7, .29, .32, .39 and .41 with its appendices) as in the eCFR version of 30 January 2026 (the brief's commuting exception is only a motor vehicle accident in the company lot, and a case reported under 1904.39 is also recorded); Crewmarrow HR, Postwren, Binderwell and Injurylog ITA (an invented stand-in for OSHA's tracking application), all invented; 7 of 10, $115,850.00 of $165,500.00, 13 of 13 traps, 0 false, 1.6 min, $0.91; short because she filed the seven entries and held the certification and the two submissions for the entries' approval; Staffnest, Mailnest and Docshelf renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a contractor's workers' compensation audit is disputed by duties and records before the carrier's window closes: a bookkeeper to clerical, a salesman to outside sales, a crew's extra overtime pay and two insured subs' costs taken off, one found only in its contract file, while an office manager on job sites, a framing estimator, a general-liability certificate and last year's audit are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 78 (B8): the New York Manual for Workers' Compensation and Employers' Liability Insurance, 2020 edition (the NCCI Basic Manual is licensed and the other bureaus' manuals could not be read from the bench machine), Rules I(J), IV, V(E) and VIII(C)(3) as they stand on 9 October 2026; Auditwell, Payroll Harbor, Binderwell and Coverwell, all invented; 5 of 5, $19,016.47, 12 of 12 traps, 0 false, 1.8 min, $0.94; Docshelf renamed before the bench, incumbent_fee_pct 50 from WC Audit Recovery's published price.

- `feat(playbooks): a small plan's late 401(k) deposits are made good with their lost earnings: two deferrals and three loan repayments deposited after the seventh business day, one found only on the loan list, while deposits on the sixth and seventh days across Labor Day, a late match, a stopped deferral, another plan's payroll and a recovery firm's share are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 77 (B8): 29 CFR 2510.3-102 (eCFR of 1 October 2026), the DOL's Voluntary Fiduciary Correction Program as amended on 15 January 2025 (self-correction from 17 March 2025), IRC 6621(a)(2) rates for 2026 and IRC 4975 as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's (c) is the welfare plan's 90 days; the pension plan's outer limit is (b)(1)); Payroll Harbor, Retireline, Correctcalc and Ledgerwren bank, all invented; 5 of 5, $87.93, 10 of 10 traps, 0 false, 1.6 min, $0.92; Ledgerline bank renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a manufacturer's COBRA election notices are mailed inside their windows from its own health plan: a layoff, a resignation, an hours cut, a death, a divorce and a child's twenty-sixth birthday, and a separated spouse at her own address, while a waiver, a cut that kept coverage, a late or missing report, a transfer, a leave, a Medicare date and a notice already mailed are left alone, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 76 (B8): ERISA 603 and 606, 29 CFR 2590.606-2, 606-3 and 606-4 and 26 CFR 54.4980B-2, -4 and -7 as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's 44 days applies to the events the employer reports; a divorce or a child's loss of status gives the administrator 14 days from the employee's letter); Crewmarrow HR, Carriercore and Postwren, all invented; 7 of 7, $770.00, 11 of 11 traps, 0 false, 1.9 min, $1.18; Staffnest and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): a laid-off analyst's three clocks are kept: unemployment filed this week, COBRA for October only to cover a scan, a marketplace plan and credit from November, and severance signed to be paid after thirty days, while a withdrawn draft, an early payment, a full COBRA, a December start and a broker's rush are left out, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 74 (B8): 45 CFR 155.420, COBRA under ERISA and 26 CFR 54.4980B, 26 CFR 1.36B-2, the OWBPA and New York Labor Law 591 and 595 as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the election deadline, 4 December, falls after the marketplace window, 29 November); Crewmarrow HR, Fernhollow Unemployment Desk, Coverfind and Postwren, all invented; 6 of 6, $6,025.00, 8 of 8 traps, 0 false, 2.3 min, $0.86; Staffnest, Joblink State and Mailnest renamed before the bench.

- `feat(playbooks): an expecting mother's leave is lined up for a caesarean birth: twelve weeks of job-protected leave, eight weeks of disability pay on her doctor's certificate and four of paid family leave from the day after it ends, while an early start, a split leave, a partner's weeks, overlapping weeks and a claim past the year are left out, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 73 (B8): Crewmarrow HR, Larkhollow Women's Health, Familyleave State, Shieldline and Postwren, all invented; 29 CFR Part 825 and New York's disability benefits and Paid Family Leave as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the brief's 825.112(a)(2) is adoption, not bonding; the 2026 maximum is 1,228.53); 3 of 3, $5,585.20, 9 of 9 traps, 0 false, 1.8 min, $1.00; Staffnest and Carenook renamed before the bench.

- `docs(readme): the tier 2 row of section 3.4 says the capture is taken before each request and filed only on a gated card for the session's own host origin` — ADR 0066: the cell no longer says a screenshot is captured before every gated proposal; it now cites the ADR.

- `docs(adr): a gated host proposal carries a capture of its tab, and the hands are tier 2` — ADR 0066 records how the capture is taken and attached, what a failed one does, the tier marker and why a page cannot set it, and the refusal of an over-cap list; ADR 0025 gains a dated note that its panel half has landed; the desktop feature doc describes the card, the capture, the tiers and the panel's tool rows.

- `fix(copy): the setup, the origins header and the apps-she-has-seen copy say what first sight and the hands now do` — ADR 0063 and ADR 0065: the setup no longer says a page with no tools cannot be acted on, the empty apps-she-has-seen list no longer says a page's tools are off, and the origins store header describes the one model of first sight (no row) instead of a shell default that writes GATED.

- `feat(tiers): the hands the shell appended are tier 2 in the daemon's manifest reply, and the panel's tool list shows them with the class the gate will apply` — README 3.4 and 3.3: lib/hands.ts marks each appended hand with the runner "shell" and strips the marker from every tool a page registered, the manifest carries it as a tool's runner, the catalog gives the entry tier 2 and /manifest reports it; a page's own tool stays tier 1; the panel lists the hands at tier 2 and shows GATED for a first-sight tab or a pinned tool.

- `feat(capture): a gated host proposal carries a capture of the focused tab the shell took before the request, and a card without one says so` — README 3.4, 3.5 and section 5's never-cut list: the shell calls page_screenshot through hands_call before each run and voice frame and sends the id as capture_id, the daemon reads it into TurnContext and the gate files it on a card only for the session's own host origin; a failed or late capture sends none and never fails the request; the card draws the capture or says why none was taken; a request whose gated_origins, gated_tools or disabled_origins list is past its cap is refused with validator_failed.

- `feat(playbooks): a planner's flexible spending account is claimed before the run-out ends: a crown's and physical therapy's share on the EOBs, two pharmacy items, a day camp and after-school care, and a card swipe answered with its EOB, while whitening, a gym, vitamins, a premium, an overnight camp, a teen's care and a child of 13 are left out, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 72: proposed Treasury Regulation 1.125-6 (72 FR 43938, 6 August 2007, read in the Government Publishing Office PDF): (a)(2) and (a)(4) an expense is incurred when the care is given and counts only in the period of coverage, (b)(3)(i) a third party's statement of date, description and amount and no self-substantiation, (b)(3)(ii) an EOB substantiates only the employee's own share, (e)(3) to (e)(6) the debit card rules and a conditional swipe pending a statement, 1.125-5(k)(2) no premiums; the grace period is 2 months and 15 days (Notice 2005-42) and a plan cannot offer it with a carryover (Notice 2013-71), both read through summaries; the plan year of 1 July 2025 to 30 June 2026 has a health FSA limit of 3,300 dollars and a carryover of 660 (Revenue Procedure 2024-40; 2026's 3,400 and 680 do not apply), and the dependent care limit of 7,500 dollars for taxable years beginning after 31 December 2025 under Public Law 119-21 section 70404 is not adopted by the world's plan, which avoids the plan year that spans 1 January; 21(b)(1) under 13, 129(c) not paid to her child under 19 (the brief's spouse is not in 129(c) as read, so no target rests on it), Publications 502 and 503 (2025); the plan, its run-out to 31 October 2026, every provider and expense are the world's own; run 1 was short (1 of 7, 6 false) because the tool descriptions did not say an expense is named by its id, so run 2 followed a world.json fix; run 2: 7 of 7, all exact, 15 of 15 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.5 min, 0 follow-ups, seven gated cards dropped, two recalls under her limit, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Corner Pharmacy (several real pharmacies) became Fennimoor Pharmacy and Ledgerline bank Ledgerwren bank; Postwren added. $900 a year; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a tester's unclaimed property is claimed free from two states under both her names: a refund under her married name at an old address, two checks and a dividend under her own, a premium refund and a policy a finder wanted a quarter of, while three namesakes, an ex-husband's, a company's, a paid one and her dead mother's are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 71: the Revised Uniform Unclaimed Property Act (2016) in the ULC text hosted by the Maine Legislature (dated 5 December 2016): 903 to 906 (a claim on the administrator's form, verified; decision within a bracketed 90 days and payment within 30; no time limit and no claimant fee in the claim sections; an action after a denial within one year), 1301 and 1302 (an agreement to locate is void from delivery to the administrator until 24 months after, and unenforceable for unconscionable compensation; the uniform text sets no percentage cap, so the brief's premise is corrected and the 10 percent is the states' own); applied as Illinois (765 ILCS 1026, section numbers prefixed 15-) and Vermont (27 V.S.A. chapter 18) enacted it, neither official code opened (read through summaries, the Vermont Treasurer's heir finder page and 74 Ill. Adm. Code 760.650), and no target rests on a state-specific number; the portals, the finder, the holders and every listing are the world's own; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.4 min, 0 follow-ups, nine dropped op lines (six gated cards, one reply with stray text after its brace, two origin-pinning refusals), one recall under her limit, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Claimit State (California's Claim It) became Heldwren Illinois and Brackenholt Vermont, Docshelf Binderwell and Mailnest Postwren. $400 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a hygienist's class action claims are filed from her own records: an air fryer, bulbs only her card shows, two kettles, earbuds by a sworn claim her card supports, pet food and a topper due tomorrow, while a late claim, an exclusion, a repeat, resale, a Toronto purchase, one she cannot show and a fee scam are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 70: Federal Rule of Civil Procedure 23 in the Cornell copy (read 10 October 2026; its history lists the 2009 order and its 23(e) is the 2018 text): 23(c)(2)(B) the notice states the right to exclusion and the binding effect, so an opt-out cannot claim, 23(e)(1)(B) notice to every member bound, 23(e)(2) approval only after a hearing and a finding of fair, reasonable and adequate, 23(e)(5)(A) objections; 28 U.S.C. 1746(2) the unsworn declaration; the FTC's refund FAQ and fake-settlement alert for the scam signs (its class-action page returned 404); every settlement, product, administrator and date is the world's own; the no-proof rule is built in as a sworn claim filed only where her card shows the purchase (earbuds) and refused where nothing does (phone cases); run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.8 min, 0 follow-ups, six gated cards dropped, two recalls cut under her limit, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Storeline (two real online shops) became Orderbrook, Cardwell Cardhollow and Mailnest Postwren; Awardhelm Payout Desk added for the scam. $200 a year, mostly minutes; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a household's recurring charges are audited: a second streaming account on the bank, a trial that became a charge, an add-on raised without notice and an unused storage plan cancelled, two refunded, while a son's and a partner's weekly use, a service used this week, a one-time purchase and a plan renewed after its reminder are left alone, across six portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 69: California Business and Professions Code 17602 in the California.Public.Law copy (statute text updated 1 January 2025; leginfo returned 403): (b)(1) and (b)(2) the trial and renewal notices, (d) and (f) cancelling online in the sign-up medium, (g)(2) a price-change notice 7 to 30 days before, (h) the annual reminder, (j) AB 2863's amendments for contracts entered, amended or extended from 1 July 2025; 17603 (goods sent without consent are a gift, goods only) and 17604 (civil remedies, no statutory refund), so each refund rests on the invented service's own terms; the FTC's click-to-cancel rule was vacated on 8 July 2025 and nothing rests on it; the services, the card, the bank and every plan are the world's own; cancellations weighted at a year of the plan and refunds at the amount; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.4 min, 0 follow-ups, six gated cards dropped, one recall at her limit, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Streamora, Fitloop and Cloudnest renamed before the bench (Streamquill, Pacewick, Vaultmoor), and Cardwell, Ledgerline bank and Mailnest (Cardhollow, Ledgerwren bank, Postwren). $600 a year, mostly minutes; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a technician's year of airline credits becomes refunds: flights moved nearly four and six and a half hours, a new arrival airport, an added stop, a lower fare class and a seat fee, while a change she made, a credit she chose, her own cancellation, a short delay, a spent credit and an uncovered flight are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 68: 14 CFR Part 260 as in the eCFR (read 10 October 2026), 260.2 (a significantly delayed or changed flight: 3 hours on a domestic and 6 on an international itinerary, a different airport, more connections, a lower class of service; a different connecting airport only for a passenger with a disability, so the roster's premise is corrected and that item is a trap), 260.4 (an ancillary fee not provided), 260.6(a) (the refund when she declines), 260.7 (a credit she chose) and 260.10 (original form of payment; 7 business days for a card); DOT's enforcement pause for renumbered flights (to 7 July 2027) is not used; the airlines, the agency, the card and every credit are the world's own; each act weighted at the fare or fee; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.6 min, 0 follow-ups, six gated cards dropped, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Skyrail and Tripfold renamed before the bench (Skylindle, Tripcairn), and Cardwell (Cardhollow); Postwren added as the mail portal. $800 a year; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a landscape architect's delayed bag is claimed: a pharmacy, toiletries on a grocery receipt, basics, a charger and a shirt, and the bag fee refunded, while food, a jacket her card paid, a dress, a new wardrobe, a companion's buys, a scarf bought after delivery, roaming and a June claim past its 21 days are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 67: Montreal Convention 1999, Article 19 (delay), Article 22(2) (the baggage limit, 1,519 SDR in force since 28 December 2024, 1,288 from 28 December 2019) and Article 31(2) to (4) (a written complaint within 21 days of the day the bag was placed at her disposal), and 14 CFR 260.5 (the checked bag fee refund, 15 hours on an international flight of 12 hours or less, in force on the world's date); the airline, its portal, the card and every receipt are the world's own; each act weighted at its amount; read across her mail, her card, her trips and the airline's claims site; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.6 min, 0 follow-ups, seven dropped op lines, no item lost; filmed. Portal name Tripfold renamed before the bench (Tripcairn), and Cardwell and Mailnest (Cardhollow, Postwren). $500 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a physical therapist's lease-end statement is checked against his lease's wear limits and the inspection, a dent, a tire, a chip, a scuff and a stain inside the limits disputed, while the mileage, the disposition fee, a long scratch, a worn tire, a cracked lens, a gouged wheel, a seat burn and the wear allowance are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 66: Regulation M, 12 CFR 1013.4(h)(2) (the lessor's wear and use standard, which must be reasonable) and (h)(3) (the motor vehicle notice and the excess mileage method), with Supplement I, as in the eCFR current to 7 October 2026, and 15 U.S.C. 1667b(a) (a lease may set wear standards that are not unreasonable); the roster cites 1013.4(l), which is the right of appraisal, and Supplement I comment 4(l)-1 says a lessee has no independent appraisal merely for liability for unreasonable wear, so the world rests only on the invented lease's own standards, price list, allowance and written dispute review; the lessor, the lease, the inspection and the statement are the world's own; each act weighted at what he gets back; read across the mail, the inspection, his photos and the lessor's site; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.1 min, 0 follow-ups, one origin-pinning refusal, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Motorvia Finance, Checkpoint Inspect, Snapshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Torvane Finance, Linestone Inspect, Lenswick photos, Postwren). $900 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a bookkeeper's repair invoices are claimed under a fuel pump recall, the labour and shop fee of one repair and a part she bought herself with its tax, while a relay fixed before the window opens, a valve, an oil change and a rotation, a warranty repair, a tow, a refunded deposit and her husband's car outside the recall are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 65: 49 CFR 573.13 as in the eCFR current to 7 October 2026 ((c) the plan's period: its start no later than one year before the manufacturer's notification to NHTSA, or the date an engineering analysis was opened if earlier, and its end no earlier than 10 days after the last owner letter was mailed; (d) the only conditions a plan may set; (e) the amount; (g) 60 days; (n) the 10-year limit) and 49 U.S.C. 30120(d), with NHTSA's owner guide; the roster's start is corrected (it runs from the notification to NHTSA, not the defect determination) and 573.13 names no claim deadline, so the world's plan sets none; the carmaker, the recall, its VIN range, its dates and its plan are the world's own; each act weighted at what she gets back; read across the mail, the recall site, the repair shop and the owner site; run 1: 4 of 4, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.6 min, 0 follow-ups, two origin-pinning refusals and one malformed recall op, no item lost; filmed. Portal names Recallcheck, Autopoint Service, Motorvia Owners and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Recallmoor, Garrowby Service, Torvane Owners, Postwren). $700 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a teacher's security deposit statement is checked against her lease and photos, six deductions contested for wear, a condition there at move-in, a rekey, a clean oven and a wall hole billed twice, while rent and water she owes, real damage, cleaning back to move-in level and an open estimate are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 64: California Civil Code 1950.5 as in California.Public.Law's copy (updated 1 January 2026; the official leginfo page could not be opened): (b) and (e) the permitted deductions and no claim for a preexisting condition or ordinary wear, (f) the initial inspection, (g) the photographs (move-out and after-repair from 1 April 2025; move-in for tenancies beginning on or after 1 July 2025), (h) the 21 days, the documents, the good-faith estimate, the 125 dollars for repairs and cleaning together and bad faith, (m) up to twice the deposit; the AB 12 cap is not in play and Granberry v. Islay Investments (1995) 9 Cal.4th 738 (a good-faith late statement does not bar the landlord's recovery) is why no target rests on lateness; the roster's lettering ((g)(1), (l)) and the premise of a per-line 125 dollars are corrected in the caveat; the landlord, the lease, the photos and the statement are the world's own; each act weighted at what she gets back; read across the mail, the portal, the photos and the small claims site; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.2 min, 0 follow-ups, no origin-pinning refusal; filmed. Portal names Rentroll tenant portal, Snapshelf photos, Courtfile and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Latchwick tenant portal, Lenswick photos, Gavelmoor claims, Postwren). $1,500 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `docs(adr): a page that names no app is catalogued under its web origin, and the hands are published for every tab` — ADR 0065 records the derived id's form (`web_<scheme>_s<host>`), the rule that `web_` is reserved so a page cannot claim another origin's id, the name-clash rule, and that a gated hand's card has no screenshot until part 2; docs/features/desktop.md gets the tiers and manifest paragraph (5c33984).

- `fix(browser): a page with no tools of its own is said to be reached through her generic hands, gated on first sight` — README 3.4 tier 2, ADR 0065: the Browser module's copy for a tool-less page no longer says she cannot act on it yet; it says she reaches it through her generic hands, gated on first sight (6bd1c1a).

- `feat(desktop): every tab with an origin is offered the nine generic hands beside its own tools, and a hand runs through hands_call` — README 3.4 tier 2, ADR 0065: the shell fetches the hands list once, publishes the nine hands for every tab with an origin (a tool-less page gets a manifest under its derived id), keeps the page's tool when a name clashes, and routes any other hand through `hands_call` in the run loop and the voice loop (2c52cde).

- `feat(desktop): every tab has one catalog id, its published app or one derived from its web origin, and a page cannot inherit a row by claiming a slug` — README 3.3, ADR 0063, ADR 0065: one function (`catalogIdOf`) keys a tab everywhere in the shell; a page with no `athena:app` is keyed by an id derived from its web origin, the `web_` prefix is reserved, and a catalog-form row applies only to the tab's own derived id (robustness-1 and robustness-3) (cc5d2e7).

- `feat(playbooks): a nurse's three credit reports are disputed with her proof, a late payment she made on time, an old collection and a bankruptcy past their limits, a paid collection and an account that is not hers, while an accurate late payment, a collection still in time, authorised inquiries and entries already fixed or being answered are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 63: 15 U.S.C. 1681i(a) (reinvestigation within 30 days, up to 15 more when she sends information inside them; the furnisher notified in 5 business days; the results notice; frivolous disputes; reinsertion), 1681s-2(b) and 1681c(a) to (c) (seven years for most adverse items, a collection's counted from 180 days after the delinquency began, ten years for a bankruptcy, and not for a report used for a transaction of $150,000 or more), as in the United States Code on 9 October 2026; the CFPB's dispute page gives the 30 days to the furnisher and none to the bureau, so the statute is followed; the bureaus, their results and their two-year display of an inquiry are the world's own; each act weighted at 125 dollars, an eighth of the roster's $1,000 a year; read across the mail, the bank and the three reports; run 1: 8 of 8, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.0 min, 0 follow-ups, no origin-pinning refusal; filmed. Portal names Ledgerscore, Creditaire, Scorewell, Ledgerline bank and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Tallowgate Credit, Pellmoor Credit, Kessingham Reports, Ledgerwren bank, Postwren). $1,000 a year, mostly minutes; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a retiree's bank account is checked under Regulation E, four unauthorised transfers reported at their capped liability and a late claim chased for its provisional credit, while a payment she sent a scammer herself, her son's fuel, a refunded and a pending charge, a subscription, a credit card charge and two claims not yet late are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 62: 12 CFR 1005.2(m) (unauthorized electronic fund transfer; Supplement I comments 2(m)-2 and 2(m)-3), 1005.6(b) ($50 inside two business days of learning of a loss, up to $500 after, the 60 days after a statement, and no tiers when no access device was lost) and 1005.11 (notice within 60 days, 10 business days or 45 with provisional credit), as in the eCFR on 9 October 2026; the CFPB's EFT FAQs are not relied on (many CFPB guidance documents were withdrawn in 2025); a credit card charge is Regulation Z, 12 CFR 1026.12(b), and is a trap; the bank, its rules, its dates and its reading of a code read out to a caller are the world's own; the weight of each act is what she gets back; read across the mail, the bank, the payment app and the card; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 12 of 12 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.6 min, 0 follow-ups, no origin-pinning refusal; filmed. Portal names Ledgerline bank, Zipcash, Cardwell and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Ledgerwren bank, Sendwren, Cardhollow, Postwren). $900 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a snack maker's recall of a supplier's almond butter is traced, three lots held and five customers told, one of them for a batch reworked into another product, while the lots either side of the range, the batches made from them, a customer given only other batches, an order not yet shipped and the distributor's own customers are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 61: 21 CFR 1.337 and 1.345 (the records of the immediate previous source and subsequent recipient), 1.361 (records within 24 hours) and 21 CFR 7.42 and 7.49 (the depth of a recall and recall communications, FDA guidance), as in force on 9 October 2026; the Food Traceability Rule, 21 CFR Part 1 Subpart S, is not applied as binding because its compliance date, 20 January 2026, was proposed to move to 20 July 2028 (Federal Register, 7 August 2025) and a November 2025 appropriations act directs FDA not to enforce it before then; the firm's procedure, its holds and its lot codes are the world's own; each act weighted at $2,500, an eighth of the roster's $20,000 an episode; read across the mail, the papers, the order system, the customers' portal and the distributor's portal; run 1: 8 of 8, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.6 min, 0 follow-ups, 8 dropped ops (origin-pinning refusals, recovered next turn, no item lost); filmed. Portal names Freshroute, Pantrylink, Docshelf, Mailnest and Ledgerline ERP renamed before the bench (Drayfold, Shelfwick, Binderwell, Postwren, Ledgerwren ERP). $20,000 an episode; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): an organic granola maker's annual update is assembled for its certifier, a new ingredient and a second warehouse reported as made and a second oat roaster as planned, two suppliers whose certification is suspended or expired reported, and the certificate numbers of three matching imports logged, while a supplier with an old paper certificate the registry shows current, an exempt distributor, a lapsed cashew grower whose cashews go only into a non-organic mix, a change already accepted, an order cancelled, a vanilla certificate that covers less than arrived, a domestic purchase and a non-organic import are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 60: 7 CFR 205.406(a) (the annual update: a summary of changes with documents, the additions and deletions planned for the coming year), 205.201(a)(3) (the plan describes how suppliers and the organic status of what is received are verified), 205.273 (each organic import accompanied by accurate NOP Import Certificate data from the Organic Integrity Database, verified against the shipment's product and quantity) and 205.101 (the exemptions that remain, among them sealed, tamper-evident packages), as in force on 9 October 2026 after the Strengthening Organic Enforcement rule (compliance from 19 March 2024); the certifier's due date, its treatment of a change already accepted and the registry Organicheck standing in for the database are the world's own; each act weighted at $75, an hour of a certifier's administrative time (Pennsylvania Certified Organic's 2021 fee sheet); read across the papers, the purchasing system and the registry; run 1: 8 of 8, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.7 min, 0 follow-ups, 1 dropped op (a registry read, not lost); filmed. Portal names Ledgerline ERP and Docshelf renamed before the bench (Ledgerwren ERP, Binderwell); Certifield Organic and Organicheck searched and kept. $2,000 a year, mostly minutes; no incumbent share.

- `feat(playbooks): a business owner's twelve IRS notices are sorted and first-time abatement is requested on the four penalties his record qualifies for, a late S corporation return, a first quarter's late deposits, a late-paid personal tax still being paid down on an installment agreement, and a late partnership return, while a second quarter, a payroll form whose history holds an earlier penalty, an accuracy-related penalty, an estimated tax penalty, the second year of a partnership whose first was waived, a penalty already removed, a tax balance and a math-error change are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 59: IRM 20.1.1.3.3.2.1 (First Time Abate) in IRM 20.1.1 as transmitted 25 November 2025 (the section dated 29 March 2023: the same return clean for the three preceding years, an estimated tax penalty excepted, a penalty an earlier first-time abate removed counted; failure-to-file, failure-to-pay and failure-to-deposit penalties covered; one period only, the earliest; the failure-to-pay penalty goes on accruing until the tax is paid), the IRS's penalty relief page reviewed 14 July 2026 (phone, letter or Form 843) and its 8 July 2026 announcement that relief is automatic for tax year 2025 and later, so every period in the world is 2024 or earlier; the older interim guidance on current filing and payment is not restated in the IRM text and no trap rests on it; the account's rules page, histories and notices are the world's own; read across the accounts, the notices, the bank and the mail; run 1: 4 of 4, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.7 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Ledgerline, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Ledgerwren bank, Binderwell, Postwren); Taxline Federal searched and kept. $1,200 an episode; no incumbent share, tax resolution firms charge flat or hourly fees.

- `feat(playbooks): a songwriter's six songs are registered with the collective so that their unmatched mechanical royalties reach her, each at her own share and matched by recording code, a song she wrote alone, three with co-writers at 50, 40 and 60 percent, an acoustic version under another title and a song a band recorded, while a namesake's recording, her cover of another's song, a work already matched, a share a publisher administers, a co-writer's song, a radio performance royalty and a song streamed only in Germany and the United Kingdom are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 58: 17 U.S.C. 115(d)(1) (the blanket licence, digital uses in the United States only), 115(d)(3)(E) (the works database: title, owner and ownership percentage) and 115(d)(3)(H) and (J) (unmatched accrued royalties held at least three years, then distributed by market share) as in force on 9 October 2026, with the collective's own help on work registration (only a title and one writer are required, shares total 100 percent, a publisher or administrator registers the share it administers) and its 30 September 2026 announcement of a first market-share distribution in April 2027 starting with January 2021 usage; the hub's rules page, its list and every portal are the world's own; read across the unmatched list, the distributor, the rights desk and the split sheets; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.1 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Tunecast, Songguild and Docshelf renamed before the bench (Releasewick distributor, Lyrebank rights desk, Binderwell). $1,500 a year; incumbent 20 percent, from a publishing administrator's published price.

- `docs(adr): ADR 0062's bot-id key was decided by the App Master headless, not by the operator` — ADR 0062 now says the Notion bot-id key was decided by the App Master headless on 2026-10-10, not by the operator (7232716).

- `docs(adr): a flat incumbent fee is not a share, and row 37's converted one is reported as written (ADR 0064)` — ADR 0064 rules that `incumbent_fee_pct` is a contingency share only, and that row 37's converted flat fee ($708 a year as 88.5%) is reported as written, with no score moved; ADR 0056 amended (02103bd).

- `feat(playbooks): an outdoor brand's twelve trademark registrations are checked against their windows and four maintained before they lapse, a section 8 with a real photograph for each of two classes, a renewal that deletes the gloves discontinued in 2023, a renewal in its grace period with both surcharges and five days left and a section 8 in its grace period, while a registration not yet in its window, one already accepted, one sold in 2024, one that lapsed in 2025, sleeping bags never sold and a discontinued snowshoe are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 57: 15 U.S.C. 1058 and 1059 and 37 CFR 2.160 (section 8 between the fifth and sixth year and in the year before each tenth, a six-month grace period), 2.161 (the goods in use, one specimen per class, goods not in use deleted, excusable nonuse only for special circumstances), 2.56 (a computer illustration, digital image or similar mock-up is not a specimen) and the USPTO's electronic per-class fees in 37 CFR 2.6(a) (325 dollars for a section 8 and for a renewal, 100 dollars for each in the grace period, 250 dollars to delete goods after a section 8 is submitted), as in force on 9 October 2026; the desk's rules page and docket are the world's own; read across the docket, the store, the papers and the mail; run 1: 4 of 4, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.0 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Markfile, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Markwick filing desk, Binderwell, Postwren). $5,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a linen brand's suspect listings are sorted across two marketplaces, copyright notices filed on four listings that copy its photographs, one by a retailer whose licence ended in June, and trademark complaints on two counterfeit aprons with the registration number and a test purchase, while an authorised retailer, a reseller of genuine towels with its own photographs, titles that only name the brand, a customer's photograph the brand does not own, a listing already removed and one on a marketplace with no portal are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 56: 17 U.S.C. 512(c)(3)(A) (the elements of a notification: a signature, the work, the infringing material, contact details, a good-faith statement and a statement under penalty of perjury), 512(f) (liability for knowingly materially misrepresenting) and 109(a) (the owner of a lawfully made copy may sell it), as in force on 9 October 2026; a complaint resting on the brand's name alone is a trademark matter and goes through each invented marketplace's own policy; the marketplaces' policies, forms and removal time are the world's own; read across the registry, the papers and two marketplaces; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 14 of 14 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.7 min, 1 follow-up (a misroute, no item lost); filmed. Portal names Marketora, Bazaarly, Brandledger and Docshelf renamed before the bench (Stallwick seller, Hawkerbrook seller, Brandwick registry, Binderwell). $10,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a ceramics shop's three card-processing statements are checked against its contract and Visa's published rates, five overcharges credited, interchange billed at the wrong card product's rate, a regulated debit billed as exempt, a markup raised by a notice that cannot reach back, a gateway fee for a merchant ID closed in 2025 and a PCI fee the contract does not name, while downgrades billed at their own rate, pass-through assessments, the fees the contract names, a credit already paid and a statement past its window are left alone, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 55: Visa USA Interchange Reimbursement Fees, Visa Supplemental Requirements, rates effective 18 April 2026 (consumer credit Card Not Present Product 1 and Non-Qualified at 3.15% + $0.10; debit CPS/e-Commerce Basic, 1.65% + $0.15 exempt and 0.05% + $0.21 regulated with 1 cent for a certified issuer) and the Federal Reserve's Regulation II as in force on 9 October 2026 (12 CFR 235.3(b), 21 cents + 5 basis points, and 235.4(a), 1 cent; the 2023 proposal to lower it is not adopted and not used); Mastercard's April 2026 schedule could not be read, so no Mastercard line is used; assessments, the settlement and address conditions for a processor's best category, its 60 days and its fees are the invented processor's own; read across the statements, the processor's notices, the contract and the bank; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 13 of 13 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.8 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Docshelf and Ledgerline renamed before the bench (Binderwell, Ledgerwren bank). $4,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a woodworker's ten open card disputes are answered with the evidence each reason code needs, delivery proved to the full address, Compelling Evidence 3.0 met with two earlier orders, a listing that matched the shelf sent, a credit already issued and a registered device, and two accepted because the board never shipped and a promised refund was never paid, while disputes the record cannot win and one past its processor's date are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 54: the Visa Core Rules and Visa Product and Service Rules of 18 April 2026 (10.4 and Compelling Evidence 3.0 as in force through 23 October 2026: two earlier undisputed transactions on the same credential more than 120 and not more than 365 days before the dispute processing date, matched on device ID or fingerprint, or IP address with the account or the full delivery address; 13.1, 13.3 and 13.6 and their responses; 30 days for the acquirer) and the Mastercard Chargeback Guide, Merchant Edition, of 13 May 2025 (4837, 45 days; a 19 May 2026 edition exists and was not readable), as they stand on 9 October 2026; the processor's date five days earlier and its $25 loss fee are the invented processor's own; read across the dashboard, the orders, the carrier's record and the mail; run 1: 7 of 7, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.8 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Shipdesk and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Parcelwick, Postwren). $8,000 a year, each acceptance weighted at the $25 fee it avoids.

- `feat(playbooks): a reseller's EU listings are brought up to the product-safety rule on two marketplaces before they are hidden, a German maker's address added, a lamp, a kettle and a diffuser made outside the Union given the Union operators the makers' mail names, a power bank given its responsible person and shears their model, while food, an antique, seeds, listings not offered in the EU, complete listings, a maker established in the Union and a throw that needs no warning are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 53: Regulation (EU) 2023/988 as published on EUR-Lex and in force on 9 October 2026 (applying from 13 December 2024, Article 52; Article 2(2) food, living plants and animals and their reproductive products, antiques; Article 4 offers targeted at consumers in the Union; Article 16 and Regulation (EU) 2019/1020 Article 4 the responsible person; Article 19 the information an offer shows, a warning only where one is affixed or supplied); no Member State's warning language is relied on; read across the product folder, the mail, and each marketplace's listings and own form; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.2 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Marketora, Bazaarly, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Stallwick seller, Hawkerbrook seller, Binderwell, Postwren). $10,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a candle maker's wholesale resale certificates are checked before the state audits them, six buyers asked for a fully completed certificate, an unsigned paper one, a blank identification number, a wrong state, a lapsed buyer and a sale with none among them, and the state's request answered with the three certificates that support it, while a Texas sale, complete certificates, a taxed sale and a buyer still inside twelve months are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 52: the Streamlined Sales and Use Tax Agreement as amended through 16 May 2024 (Section 317 A to G: 90 days, 120 days after a request, the relief lost to fraud, soliciting and entity-based exemptions, a blanket certificate through twelve months between sales; Rule 317.1 A.1, A.10 and B.8: a fully completed certificate, a signature only on paper), the Certificate of Exemption instructions and the Governing Board's member list (23 full members and Tennessee an associate, updated 26 September 2025), as they stand on 9 October 2026; read across the orders, the mail, the CRM's certificates and the state's request; run 2: 9 of 9, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.2 min, 0 follow-ups (run 1 was short, 4 of 9: Storeline's paged list declared no page parameter, fixed in the world); filmed. Portal names Clientry and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Clientwren CRM, Postwren). $15,000 a year.

- `fix(gate): a page is GATED on first sight and the user's pins reach the gate` — README §3.3, ADR 0063: A page's tools are GATED on first sight, and a tool the user pinned GATED in the Browser module now files a card. Before, a page that flagged a write readOnlyHint ran it with no card on an origin the user had never registered (32a77c5).

- `fix(connectors): a refresh is classified, runs off the lock, and a rate limit is not a lost grant` — README §4, ADR 0021: a token refresh is classified, so a 429, 5xx, timeout or token-less answer no longer costs the grant; a Gmail rate-limit 403 keeps it and tells the model with Retry-After. Refresh runs off the vault lock once per connector; the OAuth client pair is sealed only by a flow that worked; sealed files are born 0600 (e7096ed).

- `feat(desktop): the Connectors module words each seal truthfully and shows the vault's notices` — README §4, ADR 0021, ADR 0062: the Connectors module words each seal truthfully, shows the records notice as an undismissable banner, shows a connect's note, and locks the switches until connected (03c26f6).

- `fix(connectors): search_mail announces dropped reads, Notion ids are refused unless 32 hex, and Notion switches follow the bot id` — README §4, ADR 0021, ADR 0061, ADR 0062 (amended): search_mail announces dropped reads; Notion ids must be 32 hex characters; Notion switches follow the bot id (4ce716c).

- `feat(playbooks): a heavy hauler's oversize permits are bought for next week's loads across Oklahoma, Arkansas and Tennessee, an excavator permitted in each state with a rear escort where Oklahoma needs one, a start after Oklahoma City's morning curfew and a route around a bridge that failed, a transformer permitted only where it is too tall, and a culvert sent out after the Memphis rush hour, while two tanks loaded side by side, a permit Arkansas does not need and a state the transformer never enters are left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 51: 23 CFR 658.5, 658.13, 658.15 and 658.17; ODOT's size and weight permit page and OAC 730:50-5-3 and -5-18; the ArDOT Permit Rules (2023); TDOT's permit FAQ (archived February 2026) and Rule 1680-07-01, on an I-40 route through the three states, as they stand on 9 October 2026; the bridge analysis result is the world's own; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 4.0 min, 2 follow-ups (the first a misroute on 'Still open'); filmed. Portal names Oversize Online, Wideload Desk, Statepermit and Routeplan renamed before the bench (Tarnwide Oversize, Halvern Permit Desk, Fernaby Permits, Pathcairn route planner). $1,500 a year, mostly minutes, each permit weighted at a permit service's fee.

- `feat(playbooks): an owner-operator's escrow is got back from the carrier he leased to, four days after its forty-five days ran out, an ELD charged though it was returned, a washout fee and a fee for leaving that the lease never names disputed, the third quarter's interest claimed at the Treasury bill rate, and the balance demanded, while the fuel, tolls, insurance, plate and tires the lease lets it keep are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 50: 49 CFR 376.12(k)(1) to (6) and (h), (f) and (g) as they stand on 9 October 2026 (no change since 2018), with Treasury's own 13-week bill auction results (29 September 2025 to 28 September 2026) and OOIDA v. Arctic Express and v. Mayflower Transit; read across the owner-operator's papers, the carrier's settlements portal, his bank and his mail; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.1 min, 0 follow-ups; prose_audit $1,538.77 said against $2,483.77 on the cards; filmed. Portal names Haulledger, Ledgerline, Docshelf and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Settlewick settlements, Ledgerwren bank, Sheafbox, Postwren). $2,500 an episode.

- `feat(playbooks): a small carrier's driver qualification files are kept current on four clocks, a full Clearinghouse query run before its twenty-four hours run out, an overdue annual query, two overdue driving records from the right states, one record ordered for a new medical status before the paper exemption ends, and an annual review signed, while a driver with no CDL is kept out of the Clearinghouse, a driver who left is left alone and nothing done this year is done twice, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 49: 49 CFR 391.15, 391.23(m), 391.25, 391.45 and 391.51, 391.27 reserved since the Record of Violations rule (87 FR 13192, 2022), the National Registry II rule as required from 23 June 2025 with the CVSA paper exemption ending 11 October 2026 (91 FR 19255; 91 FR 64454), and 382.103, 382.701 and 382.703, as they stand on 9 October 2026; read across the ELD, the qualification files, the driving record orders and the Clearinghouse desk; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.2 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Roadlog ELD, Drivecheck, Testline and Staffnest renamed before the bench (Logmarrow ELD, Screenmoor MVR, Specimoor Clearinghouse desk, Crewmarrow HR). $3,000 a year, mostly minutes, each act weighted at $90.

- `feat(playbooks): a small Texas carrier's third-quarter fuel tax return is built from the ELD's miles and the fuel card, four jurisdiction lines with Wyoming's oil-lease road miles taken out, a cash fill counted but not credited and a receipt counted once, the totals filed and fifty-six dollars and fifteen cents paid, while Mexico, the pickup's Oklahoma run, the box truck's Louisiana delivery and a brokered Kansas load are left off, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 48: the IFTA Articles of Agreement (revised 11 March 2026: R245, R266, R820 and its 2020 Board interpretation, R830, R960.100, R1220) and Procedures Manual (January 2026: P540, P550.210, .220 and .300, P720.350 and .500), IFTA, Inc.'s third-quarter 2026 rate matrix and its 2026 distance exemption database, read from iftach.org's archived files; read across the ELD, the fuel card, the IFTA folder and the base jurisdiction's portal; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.6 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Roadlog ELD, Fueltax Online and Docshelf renamed before the bench (Odowren ELD, Tallyquart IFTA filing, Binderwell). $1,200 a quarter.

- `feat(playbooks): a garden importer's Importer Security Filings are made before each cargo is loaded, two due today, one of them on a sailing the carrier moved five days earlier, and one due on Sunday, each with the factory and the country it was made in, a filing on a flexible manufacturer and one with a new warehouse updated before arrival, while the air samples, the bulk pebbles, the Canadian container the carrier files and a rolled sailing are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 47: 19 CFR Part 149 as it stands on 9 October 2026 (149.1(a) who files and (c) bulk cargo; 149.2(a), (b), (d) and (f): the ten elements 24 hours before lading, the stuffing location and consolidator 24 hours before arrival, updates before arrival, the flexible manufacturer; 149.3(a) and (b); 149.4(a)), 19 CFR 113.62(j) ($5,000 a violation), CBP's ISF FAQ 2.0 and its mitigation guidelines; every lading and arrival in the port's time zone, UTC and Chicago time; read across the suppliers' portal, the forwarder's bookings, the import inbox and the ISF desk; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.4 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Factora, Portline and Mailnest renamed before the bench (Sourcewick, Quayloft ISF desk, Postwren). $10,000 a year, mostly minutes, each act weighted at the $5,000 a violation it protects.

- `test(connectors): a fake-provider journey connects Gmail, reads, approves a send, declines, and survives a revoke and a second account` — README §4, uat J6 (new), ADR 0062 through the routes: `tests/e2e/test_connectors_journey.py` runs six tests over `serve_scripted --fake-providers`, with the production vault built on `tests/e2e/fake_providers.py` as its transport and consent browser; it connects with a pasted client pair, reads a mail fenced, approves one send to the one allowed recipient, refuses a recipient off the list by name, records a decline as `user_denied`, shows a revoked grant as `needs_reauth`, reports an unshared Notion page as unreadable and turns writes off on a second account, and every test asserts that each vault request was answered by the fake at a pinned production host; no production code changed (4f29e7e).

- `fix(connectors): a connect as another account turns writes off and empties the allow-list, and says so` — README §4, ADR 0021 amended by ADR 0062: the connection record keeps `switches_identity`, the account its writes switch and allow-list were set under; a connect as a different account, or one whose probe names nobody, turns writes off and empties the list before the record goes live and says so in its health detail, the same account keeps them, and a record from before this that is live at load adopts its identity (fe9f0fe).

- `fix(connectors): the connections file is swapped in whole, and an unreadable one is kept and said aloud instead of read as nothing connected` — README §4, ADR 0021: the vault writes `connections.json` whole to a sibling file, flushes it and renames it over the old one, so a crash leaves the previous file; a file that cannot be read (an I/O error, invalid JSON, a top level that is not an object) is copied aside byte for byte as `connections.json.unreadable-<UTC stamp>`, and `Vault.views()` and `GET /connectors` carry a plain-words `records_notice` (8cd7ed2).

- `feat(playbooks): a sensor maker's export orders are screened before they ship, two near-matches to listed names cleared on address, country and identifier and an unverified buyer with a sound statement released, while a buyer listed under another name, a denied company, an unverified one whose statement falls short, a company its sanctioned owner blocks and a forwarder that will not name its buyer are held, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 46: the EAR as they stand on 9 October 2026 (15 CFR 744.11 and Supplement No. 4 to Part 744; 736.2(b)(4) and Supplement No. 1 to Part 764; 744.15, Supplement No. 6 to Part 744 and 758.1(b)(8); Supplement No. 3 to Part 732; 764.2(g)), OFAC's SDN list and its 50 Percent Rule (13 August 2014), trade.gov's Consolidated Screening List, and BIS's affiliates rule (90 FR 47201) as stayed by 90 FR 50857 until 9 November 2026; read across the storefront's orders, the screening tool's matches, hand searches and guidance, the compliance inbox and the ERP's policy and customer master; five orders released and five held, an unlisted affiliate the stayed rule does not reach left to the person; run 1: 10 of 10, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.9 min, 0 follow-ups; filmed. Portal name Orderloft renamed before the bench (Orderwick). $0 a year, mostly minutes (about 60 h), each act a $1 count weight.

- `feat(playbooks): a kitchenware importer's misclassified entries are put right the way each can still be, corrected before liquidation where the window is open and protested within a hundred and eighty days after it, one five days from its deadline, while the platters its own ruling classifies, an entry out of time and one that must wait for liquidation are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 45: 19 U.S.C. 1514 and 19 CFR Part 174 (a protest within 180 days after, but not before, liquidation), CBP's post-summary correction test under 19 CFR 101.9(b) as republished at 91 FR 41053 (6 July 2026, operational 5 August 2026: within 300 days of entry or up to 15 days before the scheduled liquidation, whichever is earlier), 19 U.S.C. 1504 and 19 CFR 159.11, 19 U.S.C. 1505, the HTSUS (2026) General Rules of Interpretation and 19 CFR 177.9, as they stand on 9 October 2026; read across the ERP's product master and duty ledger, the specifications, an invented ruling and tariff extracts, the broker's entries and notes and the importer's trade account with CBP; two post-summary corrections and two protests at 3924.10.40; the ruled platters, a protest out of time, an entry that must wait for liquidation and the entries already right left alone; run 1: 4 of 4, all exact, 14 of 14 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.1 min; her closing words named $6,570 against the $10,998 filed; filmed. Authored and benched by B5a (1e304751), committed unchanged. $30,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a lens maker's late overnight parcels are claimed under each carrier's money-back guarantee, inside each carrier's own window, for the transportation charge alone, while the services whose guarantee is suspended, a storm, a missing suite number, a late drop-off, a signature nobody could give and a parcel that was on time are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 44: two invented carriers' money-back guarantees, modelled on the UPS Tariff/Terms of 17 April 2026 (section 54: guaranteed air services, all others suspended, 15 days from the scheduled delivery, transportation charges only) and the FedEx Service Guide of 2026 updated 11 September (guaranteed overnight and 2Day A.M. from 12 February 2026, all others suspended, 15 days from the invoice date, one refund per package, the weather, address and signature exceptions), with USPS's Priority Mail Express refund (DMM 604.9.5), as published on 9 October 2026; read against the shipping app's tender, address and signature records, the inbox and each carrier's guide, alerts, invoices and credits; three refunds requested at Wrenpost and three claimed at Lanecourier; nine excepted, suspended, on-time, credited or out-of-window parcels left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 11 of 11 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.7 min, 3 follow-ups; filmed. Portal names Swiftpost and Shipdesk renamed before the bench (Wrenpost, Labelyard). $4,000 a year.
