- `feat(playbooks): an importer's ocean demurrage and detention invoices are held to the federal billing rule, two invoices issued too late and one missing what it must state disputed in full, the days a storm closed the terminal and the empties were refused taken off, and free time the contract gives put back, while the charges that are right are left to pay, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 43: the FMC's Demurrage and Detention Billing Requirements, 46 CFR Part 541 (in force since 28 May 2024: 541.7's 30 days from the last day charged, 541.6's required contents, 541.5's no obligation to pay an invoice that leaves one out, 541.8's 30 days to ask and 30 to answer; 541.4 on who may be billed set aside in World Shipping Council v. FMC and removed on 29 December 2025, so not applied), the interpretive rule at 46 CFR 545.5 and 46 U.S.C. 41104, as they stand on 9 October 2026, with each invented carrier's tariff and the service contract on their portals; read against the forwarder's containers and contract terms, the terminal's availability record and notices, and the inbox; four Halvard disputes and two Trident waiver requests filed at the amounts not owed; the trucker's and the warehouse's delays, the contract's free time applied, a waived and a paid invoice left alone, and nothing unowed paid; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 4.9 min; filmed. Portal names Meridian Lines and Portwatch renamed before the bench (Halvard Lines, Berthgate). $20,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a lighting maker's freight claims are filed with the carriers that moved the goods, inside nine months, with concealed damage behind a clean receipt proved, a released-value crate claimed at its limit and a pallet lost in January claimed three days before its time runs out, while damage its own packing or its customer caused is left alone, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 42: the Carmack Amendment, 49 U.S.C. 14706 (actual loss; the receiving and delivering carrier; a released value by the shipper's written declaration; no less than 9 months to file and 2 years to sue), 49 CFR 370.3, 370.5, 370.7 and 370.9 (what a claim is and is not; 30 days to acknowledge; 120 to decide, then status every 60) and Missouri Pacific R. Co. v. Elmore & Stahl (1964), as they stand on 9 October 2026, with each invented carrier's own bill of lading terms and tariff on its portal; read against the TMS's shipments and receipts, the claims inbox, the invoices, packing records and inspection reports; two damage claims, the concealed damage, the chandelier at $170, the short carton and the lost pallet filed with the carrier that moved each; a December shortage out of time, damage from its own packing and from the customer's forklift, another carrier's shipment and two open claims left alone; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 17 of 17 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.9 min; filmed. Portal names Ridgeway Freight and Bluepeak LTL renamed before the bench (Harrowgate Freight, Larkfield LTL). $18,000 a year.

- `fix(connectors): a line break in a mail subject is refused by name instead of ending the turn` — README §4, ADR 0021: the Gmail provider raises `SubjectRefused` before any request when a `send_mail` subject carries CR or LF, and `Service.call` returns it as a `validator_failed` failure naming the line breaks; any other `ValueError` from a provider becomes an `engine_error` failure that says the request could not be built, instead of leaving the turn (5bd9e0e).

- `fix(connectors): the transport follows no redirect, so the credential stays on the pinned host` — README §4, ADR 0021: the vault's `UrllibTransport` opens through an opener whose redirect handler follows nothing, so a 3xx is returned as the answer and the credential never goes to a host the pin did not name; a test with two loopback servers finds the second receives nothing (b2bc590).

- `fix(connectors): a recipient item is exactly one address, checked and sent as the same address` — ADR 0061: `connectors/addresses.one_address` takes a `to` or `cc` item to its one bare address, or to nothing when it parses to several or none or carries CR or LF; `check_egress` refuses such an item by name before the allow-list, and the Gmail provider builds its `To` and `Cc` headers and its reply only from the checked addresses (ee1e4ae).

- `fix(approvals): a card whose replay was refused can be approved again or declined, instead of being dead until the agent is asked again` — ADR 0060: when the replay of an approved card was refused (its app switched off), approving again replays it if the row is still approved, unspent and unexpired, and `Approvals.decline_approved` closes it with one conditional update and the usual `user_denied` episode and row; policy still runs first and `consume` still decides single use, and the panel says to switch the app back on and approve again (c31ef60).

- `feat(playbooks): a married name is carried through Social Security, the licence, the passport, the bank and payroll in the order each office needs, on the free passport form, with a REAL ID before Thanksgiving and a husband's change started where it has to start, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 41: Social Security's name change on Form SS-5 (POMS RM 10212.015 and RM 10212.055; the online-only service for participating states, RM 10205.630, and the start-online, finish-in-office application within 45 days, RM 10205.635), the REAL ID rule enforced since 7 May 2025 (6 CFR 37.5, 37.11 and 37.13(b)(2)) with TSA's $45 ConfirmID, the passport name change by DS-5504 with no fee within a year of issue against DS-82 and DS-11 and online renewal closed to it (22 CFR 22.1, 51.21 and 51.54), and IRS Publications 15 (2026) and 1281, as they stand on 9 October 2026, with the invented state's own licence rules on its portal; read against both Social Security records, both licences, her passport, the bank's accounts and policies and her payroll profile and trip; his SS-5 started with its office visit, her licence visit booked as NC-RID, the DS-5504 prepared, her two accounts renamed and payroll changed; a second SS-5, his licence visit and account, the dearer forms, her company's account, her ticket and a $129 concierge left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.8 min, 1 follow-up; filmed. $0 an episode, mostly minutes (about 10 h), each act weighted at $6.66.

- `feat(playbooks): a mother's hospital stay, changed from inpatient to observation the day after she was admitted, is appealed to Medicare's quality reviewers so the ten thousand two hundred dollars her nursing home billed privately can fall to Part A, and the stays the rule does not reach are left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 40: 42 CFR 405.1210 to 405.1212 (the change-of-status appeal created after Alexander v. Azar, final rule CMS-4204-F, 89 FR 83240, 15 October 2024, with the Medicare Change of Status Notice and the QIO process in use since 14 February 2025, its expedited path before release and its standard path after it with no deadline, and the retrospective window for 2009 to 2025 stays closed on 2 January 2026), 42 CFR 409.30 (three consecutive inpatient days, the admission day counted and the discharge day not, observation days never), 409.60, the NOTICE Act's MOON (489.20(y)) and 405.942, as they stand on 9 October 2026; read against the stays' status orders and notices, the hospital's billing, the Medicare record and claims, and the nursing home's statements and policy; the September change of status asked of the QIO as a standard request on its 14 September order and 4 days, and the $10,200 statement held; the March stay that was observation from the start, the closed 2024 window, the June stay with its inpatient days, redeterminations, both bills and a 30% firm left alone; run 1: 2 of 2, both exact, 12 of 12 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.9 min; filmed. $10,000 an episode.

- `feat(playbooks): a mother living on her Social Security is applied for the Medicare Savings Program her income really fits, with only the savings the rules count, and gets Extra Help with it, while a father who already has it is left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 39: section 1905(p) of the Social Security Act with SSI's income and resource rules (20 CFR 416.1123(b)(2), 416.1124(c)(12), 416.1207, 416.1212, 416.1216, 416.1218, 416.1230 and 416.1231) and section 1860D-14 with 42 CFR 423.773 and 423.774, as they stand on 9 October 2026, with CMS's 2026 figures (the $15,960 poverty guideline; $1,350, $1,616 and $1,816 with the $20 disregard; $9,950 and $14,910 resources; Extra Help's $16,590 and $18,090) and an invented state on the federal floor; read against the mother's forwarded letters, her bank, both parents' Medicare records and her saved draft; QMB applied for at $1,328 countable income and $8,516.25 countable resources, with the checking, the savings and the countable part of the burial fund on the resource statement; SLMB, QI and QDWI, the car, the home, the furniture, the plot, the life policy's cash value, Extra Help for the deemed father and a $189 filing service left alone; run 1: 4 of 4, all exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.8 min; filmed. $2,500 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a mother's required minimum distributions are taken at two custodians, each from the account it must come from, the year she missed from the IRA she inherited corrected with the waiver asked for, and nothing taken that she does not owe, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 38: IRC 401(a)(9) with the July 2024 final regulations (T.D. 10001, applying from 2025: 1.401(a)(9)-5 and -9, 1.408-8, 54.4974-1), the RMD at 73 for her 1951 birth year and the 1 April required beginning date that put two RMDs in 2025, Notices 2022-53, 2023-54 and 2024-35 relieving 2021 to 2024 for a 10-year-rule beneficiary of an owner who died after his required beginning date and annual RMDs required from 2025, and IRC 4974 (25%, 10% when corrected in the window that ends 31 December 2027, the reasonable-error waiver on Form 5329) as they stand on 9 October 2026; read against the preparer's drafts, both custodians' 31 December values and schedules and the mother's wishes; the 2026 RMDs requested from her Pinebrook IRA and the inherited IRA, the missed 2025 one corrected from the inherited IRA, the double automatic withdrawal stopped, and the 2025 Form 5329 approved with the waiver; the Roth, the 401(k), her own IRA in the inherited IRA's place, retitling, the 2024 form and the drafted taxes left alone; run 1: 5 of 5, all exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 4.3 min, 1 follow-up; filmed. $2,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a father's caregivers are kept on the right side of the household employer rules, a contractor made an employee before his first pay, his I-9 signed the day it is due, a W-2 number put right, and the state's third-quarter report filed and paid on the wages it covers, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 37: IRS Publication 926 for 2026 (the $3,000 cash-wage threshold, which SSA's 2026 notice also fixes, up from 2025's $2,800; FUTA from $1,000 in a quarter; the family exemptions, the employer's child under 21 among them; control decides employee or not, and an agency's worker is the agency's; the EIN, the W-2 and W-3 by Monday 1 February 2027, Schedule H, withholding or 1040-ES estimates; the name and number as on the card; Form I-9 in three business days under 8 CFR 274a.2) as it stands on 9 October 2026, with the invented state's own unemployment rules stated on its portal; read against the timesheets, the payroll set-ups, the bank and the state account; the evening caregiver made a W-2 employee, his I-9 signed today, a W-2 number corrected, the third quarter filed at $8,418 covered and $4,408 taxable and paid at $149.87, and the new hire reported; the agency's aide, the exempt son, quarters already filed or not yet ended, the September estimate already paid and a $195 filing service left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, all exact, 16 of 16 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.5 min; filmed. $800 a year, mostly minutes.

- `feat(playbooks): Part B taken up through the special period the month a job ends, starting the day the employer coverage stops, with no lifetime penalty, and a spouse on retiree coverage told why she must wait, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 36: 42 CFR 406.24 and 407.20 (the special enrollment period for group health plan coverage based on current employment, any size of employer, 8 months), 406.24(e) (the start date), 407.15, and 408.22 and 408.24 (10% for each full 12 months, current-employment months excluded) as they stand on 9 October 2026, with Forms CMS-40B and CMS-L564, Social Security's rule that COBRA and retiree coverage are not current employment, and creditable coverage kept to Part D; read against HR's packet, the COBRA notice, a broker's wrong advice, the employment record, the household's coverage and both Medicare records; the L564 asked of HR and Part B from 1 November 2026; an enrollment for the wife, COBRA, early cancellations, an L564 for the son and a $299 filing service left alone; run 1: 2 of 2, both exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.7 min; one checkpoint line dropped as invalid JSON; filmed. $1,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): two parents' drug plans tested in open enrollment on what they really take at the pharmacy they really use, and each moved to the plan that costs them least for the year, not the lowest premium, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 35: the annual election period of 42 CFR 423.38(b), with 423.40, 423.32(e), 423.120(b)(3) and 423.124, and CMS's 2027 $700 deductible limit and $2,400 out-of-pocket threshold, in a world dated Monday 2 November 2026, inside the period; each invented plan's formulary, tiers, prior authorization, network, preferred pharmacies, premium and deductible are its own; read against the doctor's medication lists and notes, the pharmacy's fills and home delivery, the current plan's annual notice and four plans' terms and formularies; the mother enrolled in Northstar and the father in Keystone; the lowest-premium plan on a stale estimate, the plan that drops the father's inhaler, a dearer plan for the mother, early disenrollment and moving prescriptions left alone; run 1: 2 of 2, $1,516, every card exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.3 min; filmed. $1,200 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a mother's Social Security kept the way a representative payee must, her care paid first, the payee paid back only for what she bought her, the money that left for someone else returned, and the year's report filed to the dollar, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 34: 20 CFR 404.2035, 404.2040, 404.2040a, 404.2045 and 404.2065 and Form SSA-623 as they stand on 9 October 2026, with the 2018 Act's four exemptions, which do not reach an adult child, read against a year of the payee account's statements, the savings, the care home's agreement and invoices, the payee's receipts and the benefit payments; October's board and care paid, a charge her care level includes disputed, two receipts for the mother paid back, the payee's phone bill put back, and the report filed at $20,976, $18,600, $1,011 and $3,246.20; an exemption, a paid bill, the payee's guest meals, groceries and time, and a receipt already paid back left alone; no SSI, so no resource-limit trap; run 1: 6 of 6, every card exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.2 min; one op line dropped as invalid JSON and re-sent; filmed. $500 a year, mostly minutes.

- `feat(playbooks): a veteran father's burial allowance claimed two days before its two years run out, with the plot allowance and the government marker his cemetery allows, and nothing his record does not support, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 33: 38 U.S.C. 2303 and 2307 and 38 CFR 3.1700 to 3.1713 as they stand on 9 October 2026 (the non-service-connected allowance received within 2 years of burial under 3.1703(a)(1), no other time limit; no presumption of a service-connected death for a total rating based on unemployability, 3.1704(b)), VA's amounts for the date of death ($978 each for a death from 1 October 2024), 38 CFR 38.630 for the marker and 14.636 against claim fees, read against the DD-214, the death certificate, the rating decision, the cemetery's section rules, the funeral statement and the son's payments; the burial and plot allowances claimed at $978 and the flat bronze marker the section allows; a service-connected allowance, the VA-care allowance, forbidden markers, a medallion for an unmarked grave, the funeral home's markers and a $395 filing fee left alone; run 1: 3 of 3, $3,196, every card exact, 11 of 11 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.7 min; filmed. $2,000 an episode.

- `feat(playbooks): a mother's direct cremation priced at three funeral homes, every item the Funeral Rule says a home cannot require declined, and the home that is cheapest all in booked, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 32: the FTC Funeral Rule, 16 CFR Part 453, as it stands on 9 October 2026, unchanged since 1994 (the 2022 advance notice and 2023 workshop on online prices led to no rule), read against three homes' price lists and unsigned statements, the mother's letter, the family's decision and the urn receipt; a casket said to be required for a direct cremation declined for the alternative container, embalming said to be required by law and an urn handling fee declined, and the home cheapest once corrected, counting the release charge of the home that holds her, booked; the basic services fee, the medical examiner's authorization, refrigeration already provided, the home that only looked cheapest as quoted, the dearest home and a 25% advocate left alone; run 1: 4 of 4, $2,115, every card exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.7 min; filmed. $2,500 an episode.

- `fix(approvals): an approved card and a spoken turn carry the switched-off apps list, so the replay re-checks the switch` — the desktop's `DaemonApi.resolve` always posts `disabled_origins`, and `run.ts`'s answer flow passes the turn's own list; the voice client's start and text frames carry it; the voice gateway reads it with `routes.disabled_origins_from`, passes it to `turn_context` for a spoken turn and puts it on a spoken approval's decision body; an approval with the app switched off is refused `409 foreign_origin` and ledgered, with no execute (1c56826).

- `fix(harness): a ledger write that raises is retried once and no longer ends a turn without a terminal event` — README §2 invariant 6: `RoundHarness._finish` is the one guarded row write, tried once more on a raise and then returning the exception's type name instead of raising; a success whose row could not be written ends in `TurnError(reason='unknown')` rather than a summary, the cancel path still re-raises, and the turn's setup now runs inside the guarded region, so a raise there gets a row and an error (024cdec).

- `fix(harness): every exit of a turn writes its one ledger row, a raise and a closed stream included` — README §2 invariant 6: `run_turn` writes a row with reason `unknown` and raises a `TurnError` naming only the exception's type when the round loop raises, and writes a `cancelled` row and re-raises when the stream is closed or cancelled; `CliHarness` turns an over-long CLI line into an `engine_error`, `SubprocessTransport` reads with an 8 MiB limit, and `LedgerHook` marks a turn recorded only once the write returns, so a failed write can be retried (b49a255).

- `fix(proving): the proving server's child no longer inherits ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, so both its spawns bill the subscription` — `Runner.start` builds the child's environment through `roles.claude_env` and then drops the judge token; a test runs a fake child with the key set and finds it absent (d3ebfe4).

- `fix(harness): no spawn of an engine CLI inherits ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, so the product's claude_code path bills the subscription too` — `child_env` in `harness/transports.py` drops the key from both the inherited environment and the request's own, `SubprocessTransport.run` spawns with it, `roles.claude_env` returns it so the rule lives in one place, and ADR 0007 gets a dated note (bd41162).

- `fix(proving): a claude control role no longer inherits ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, so it bills the subscription` — `roles.claude_env` drops the key and keeps the rest of the environment, and `subprocess_runner` passes it to `claude -p` (f653d1c).

- `fix(playbooks): evidence --verify reports a malformed bench.json or evidence.json instead of crashing` — such a playbook stands as `unreadable`, with a note naming the file and the problem; every other playbook is still reported, and `--verify` exits 1 (cb73d4f).

- `test(playbooks): the bench's cap stop and its model-turn error stop each have a test` — through the existing `scripted_model` seam: two turns and then `cap: spent $1.20 of $1.00` with no third turn, and one turn ending on `engine_error: scripted failure`; `bench.py` unchanged (ed9af69).

- `feat(playbooks): check refuses a playbook without a caveat, and the three written before the roster now carry one` — `playbook.json` needs a non-empty string `caveat`, for every playbook; ADR 0057 rule 4 notes it; `cpg-deductions`, `fba-reimbursements` and `medical-bills` name their invented actors, the public rules and their date, and say it is not advice, and are filmed again (their bench is untouched).

- `feat(playbooks): the Playbooks panel says its numbers were measured once on an invented portal world, and marks the author's estimates as estimates` — a standing line under the header badge and on the bench glance, "on the bench" on every measured figure, "planted in its world" for the sum there was to find, "author's estimate" on the tile's value and on the minutes by hand, and the caveat's first sentence on each tile; the full council's must-address on playbook-execution.

- `test(playbooks): every subcontractor's insurance check re-benched after ADR 0059, and it exceeds, 9 of 9 where it was 4 of 9` — ADR 0059's consequences: 9 of 9, $7,200 of $7,200, every card exact, 11 of 11 traps avoided, 0 false, no op dropped, 3.2 min, $1.96; the run before was short, 4 of 9, $3,200, with five deficiency notices dropped for a stray quote after the closing brace; playbook, world and truth unchanged; filmed again.

- `feat(playbooks): every out-of-network therapy session claimed with the right code, every overdue claim chased, and every one processed wrong appealed in time, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 31: 29 CFR 2560.503-1 for post-service claims as it stands on 9 October 2026 (30 days, one 15-day extension on notice, 180 days to appeal), with the invented plan's own out-of-network benefit, deductible, allowed amounts and 12-month filing limit, and the No Surprises Act applied only as far as it reaches (not an out-of-network therapist the patient chose); three unclaimed sessions submitted (one as 90834 for its 45 minutes, as the therapist's office corrected), a claim past its 30 days with no extension notice chased, and appeals of last year's allowed amounts and of a deductible already met; a session past the filing limit, four already claimed, a late-cancellation fee, couples counseling, appeals of claims paid right, past their window or pending under a valid extension, surprise-billing complaints and a 15% company left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, every card exact, 14 of 14 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.8 min; two ops dropped in the inbox's turn (a JSON envelope with prose after it, then an op with no action), so she spent all 3 follow-ups going back; filmed. $3,000 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): every record a second opinion needs requested from five providers, at the fee the rule allows, and the late one chased, across five portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 30: HIPAA's right of access, 45 CFR 164.524, as it stands on 9 October 2026 (30 days with one 30-day extension on written notice, HHS's 2021 15-day proposal not finalized; a reasonable, cost-based fee with no search or retrieval charge, applied since Ciox Health v. Azar, D.D.C. 2020, only to the patient's own request), read against the clinic's list, the providers' messages, the hospital's schedule and invoices, the imaging studies and the fax contacts; her own notes and labs requested, a $109 invoice disputed to the hospital's $6.50 flat fee, a fair itemized paper copy paid, her own images requested by birth date, the orthopedic practice faxed at its new number, and the therapy clinic chased past its 30 days; a spouse's chart, a dispute of the insurer's third-party copy and of the fair invoice, the overcharge paid, a namesake's images, a reassigned fax number, a $199 service and a look-alike demand left alone; run 1: 7 of 7, every card exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.6 min; filmed. $150 an episode, mostly minutes (about 8 hours).

- `feat(playbooks): a parent's drug plan asked for every formulary and tier exception his history supports, with the prescriber's statement, and none it does not, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 29: 42 CFR 423.578 with the timeframes of 423.568(b) and 423.570(d) (72 hours standard, 24 expedited, from receipt of the prescriber's statement) and section 1860D-2(e)'s exclusions as they stand on 9 October 2026, read against the plan's drug list, the doctor's medication history and statements, and the pharmacy's rejections and days of supply; a formulary exception on a signed statement, one expedited where the doctor says waiting may seriously jeopardize his health, and the doctor asked for the tiering statement her note offers; a step therapy drug never tried, a specialty-tier tiering request, an exception already approved for the plan year, a hair-growth drug, a brand at the generics-only tier's price, statements for those and needless cash fills left alone; run 1: 3 of 3, every card exact, 15 of 15 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.7 min; one op line dropped (a JSON envelope with a stray closing tag after it) and re-sent in the same turn; filmed. $2,400 a year.

- `feat(playbooks): a nonprofit hospital's charity care applied for on every account that qualifies, with the household's income as its own policy counts it, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 28: IRC 501(r) and 26 CFR 1.501(r)-1 to -6 as they stand on 9 October 2026 (240 days from the first post-discharge statement to apply; no extraordinary collection action before 120 days; actions suspended once an application is in), with the household and household income as the invented hospital's policy defines them, read against the accounts, the tax preparer's summary, three months of pay stubs and the joint account's deposits; three accounts applied for as a household of 4 at $65,760 a year, under the $66,000 free-care line, and the collector told before it reports the stay to the credit bureaus; an account already approved, a cosmetic surgery, an independent physicians' group, a closed account, payments while pending, a 30% advocate and a look-alike demand left alone; run 1: 4 of 4, every card exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.5 min; filmed. $6,000 an episode.

- `feat(playbooks): every Medicare denial billed to the patient only where a valid ABN was signed, and refunded where it was not, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 27: Social Security Act sections 1879 and 1842(l) and CMS's ABN instructions (MLN006266, May 2025) as they stand on 9 October 2026, with form CMS-R-131 in its version approved 13 March 2026 (expiring 31 March 2029) and the old form accepted only until 12 May 2026; read against the denied lines, Novabridge's remittance codes, the scanned ABNs and the patient payments; two lines with valid notices and a routine physical excluded by law billed, and four payments refunded where the notice was generic, signed after the draw, on the outdated form or the routine check-in copy; those four lines, an MUE unit, a line Medicare paid, a GZ line with no notice, a valid notice already paid at the desk and that payment, coinsurance and a refund already sent left alone; run 1: 7 of 7, $539, every card exact, 11 of 11 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.0 min; filmed. $1,800 a month.

- `feat(playbooks): every uninsured and self-pay patient's good faith estimate sent on time, and every bill $400 over its estimate caught before it goes out, across three portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 26: 45 CFR 149.610 and 149.620 as they stand on 9 October 2026, with HHS's enforcement discretion on co-provider and co-facility items (FAQ Part 3, 2 December 2022, pending further rulemaking) still in place, so the roster's 'co-provider items belong on the estimate' is not authored as an obligation and the trap is a co-provider's bill counted against the clinic's estimate instead; five estimates issued by their business-day deadlines (one revised when an X-ray was added before the visit, one for an insured patient who will not file a claim, the lab's pathology left to the lab), two statements $410 and $445 over their estimates held, and the dispute entity's notice answered with the estimate and the bill; an estimate reissued after the visit to match the bill, one for a cancelled visit, statements $375 over, over only by the lab's own bill, or over a replaced estimate, an insured patient's coinsurance, a disputed bill in the collections queue and a 3% vendor left alone; run 1: 8 of 8, every card exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.9 min; filmed. $1,200 a year, mostly minutes: each act is weighted at $50. B3's first half is complete.

- `feat(playbooks): every clinician's Medicare revalidation, credentialing attestation and license kept current, on four clocks in four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 25: 42 CFR 424.515 (revalidation every five years) and 424.57(e) (every three for a DMEPOS supplier) with CMS's posted due dates and seven-month window, CAQH-style re-attestation every 120 days, and an invented board's 60-day renewal window and CME hours, read against the credentialing folder and the clinic's quarterly calendar; three revalidations submitted (the DMEPOS enrollment due in November on its three-year cycle), a renewed liability certificate uploaded and two profiles attested (one already lapsed), and two licenses renewed on the hours their transcripts show; a TBD due date, a date more than seven months out, a group application in review, a physician who left (revalidation, attestation and license), a license 12 hours short, a renewal window not yet open, last year's certificate and an unbound quote left alone; two runs, the world fixed once (it said to replace the expired certificate before attesting, so she held the attestation behind the unsigned upload); run 2: 8 of 8, every card exact, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.6 min; filmed. $3,000 a year, mostly minutes: each act is weighted at $150.

- `feat(playbooks): every therapy authorisation renewed before visits run past it, each on its own payer's clock, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 24: CMS-0057-F's decision timeframes (7 calendar days standard, 72 hours expedited) applied from 1 January 2026 only to the payer types it names, a Medicare Advantage plan and a Medicaid managed care plan from its 1 July 2026 rating period, while a self-funded employer plan and a federal-exchange plan keep their own 15 days (29 CFR 2560.503-1, 45 CFR 147.136) and a fax-only payer its 10 business days; each patient's first visit past the authorisation, by date or visits used, read against each portal's acceptance window; two continuations, two renewals and a fax sent today because next Friday would be too late; requests the portals would not yet accept, one next week's run has time for, a renewal pending, a discharge, Original Medicare (no prior authorisation for outpatient therapy) and faxes to portal-only payers left alone; run 1: 5 of 5, every request exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.4 min; filmed. $1,500 a month, mostly minutes.

- `feat(playbooks): every credit balance traced to whose money it is, with Medicare overpayments returned inside 60 days, patients refunded and posting errors corrected rather than refunded, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 23: Social Security Act 1128J(d) and 42 CFR 401.305 as amended from 1 January 2025 by the CY 2025 Physician Fee Schedule final rule (identified on actual knowledge, reckless disregard or deliberate ignorance; the 60 days suspended for up to 180 days during a timely, good-faith investigation of related overpayments) as the Medicare contractor states them, read against the credit balances' posting histories, the compliance log, the bank's deposits and offsets and the patient payments; a duplicate Medicare payment returned before its 60 days end on 13 October, a 14-claim coding audit returned with 60 days from its investigation's close on 28 September, an ECG not performed returned, three patients refunded (a preventive visit's coinsurance, a balance paid twice, a coinsurance the Medigap plan also paid), and an unposted offset, a transposed account and a remittance posted twice corrected; refunds of the offset, the double posting and the misposted payment, a payer's money refunded to patients, a deposit for next week's procedure and reversals of real payments left alone; run 1: 9 of 9, every card exact, 13 of 13 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.9 min; filmed. The value is money returned or refunded on time, not recovered.

- `feat(playbooks): every line paid short of the payer contract found and disputed, and the bundled lines left alone, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 22: two invented payer agreements, their fee schedules and an amendment read against the posted lines and the 835 remittances' adjustment codes, under CMS's NCCI edits (a bundled line is not a line paid short); disputes for a surgeon paid at the non-participating rate after being credentialed, an amendment's rate missed on two lines (one window closing on 14 October), an X-ray cut as a multiple procedure the contract limits to surgical codes, and last year's rate; two NCCI-bundled lines, a visit with no modifier 25, a deductible, a sequestration cut, the lesser-of-billed rule, two lines whose windows closed, a line already in dispute and three lines paid right left alone; run 1: 5 of 5, $565, every dispute exact, 12 of 12 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.5 min; filmed. B3 begins.

- `feat(harness): a stray quote after a closing brace is the grammar's seventh repair` — ref §3.2 steps 3 and 4, ADR 0059: `stray_quote` drops a `"` outside string literals whose previous significant character is `}` or `]` and whose next is `,`, `}` or `]`, and runs first among the text repairs; `REPAIRS` is a closed list of seven. Found by row 17 (sub-insurance-certs), whose turn 12 lost five of six deficiency notices to it.

- `feat(playbooks): voucher payments kept coming, with inspections cured, tenant damage reported and rent increases asked for on time, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 21: the Housing Choice Voucher program (24 CFR part 982) as the housing authority states it, with units inspected under HQS (24 CFR 982.401) until the voucher programs' NSPIRE compliance date of 1 February 2027, read against the leases, the work orders and tenant tickets and the bank's deposits; a missing smoke alarm dispatched as an emergency inside its 24 hours and two owner repairs dispatched, a range replaced after its deadline certified so the abated payment resumes and a fan certified before its deadline, a child's baseball and a punched door reported as the families' with their evidence, and two rent increases asked for inside their ranges more than 60 days ahead; work orders for repairs already done and a unit that passed, certifications of work not done, a worn latch and an owner's GFCI blamed on tenants, an increase 53 days out, one in a lease's first year and one for a market-rate tenant left alone; run 1: 9 of 9, every card exact, 13 of 13 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.4 min; filmed. B2 is complete.

- `feat(playbooks): the escrow analysis checked against the real tax and insurance bills, and the surplus claimed, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 20: RESPA section 10 and Regulation X (12 CFR 1024.17 and 1024.35) as the servicer states them, read against the tax collector's bills and payment dates and the insurer's premiums and payments; a tax line carried forward past the real bill and last year's premium disputed at the amounts the bills show, a three-month cushion cut to a sixth of the corrected disbursements, the late penalty the servicer charged to escrow disputed, the $214.60 surplus claimed inside its 30 days, and the card's second payment of a premium refunded; three right projections, four right payments, the escrow's own premium payment, an umbrella premium, a flood payment, a 35% audit firm and a $149 tax-relief firm left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, every card exact, 12 of 12 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.2 min; filmed.

- `feat(playbooks): mortgage insurance taken off as soon as the law allows, on the household's loan and a parent's, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 19: the Homeowners Protection Act (12 U.S.C. 4901 and 4902) and Regulation X's notice of error as the servicer states them, read against two years of payment history, the bank's payments, the recorded liens and the closing disclosure; a request on the household's loan, below 80% of the $410,000 sale price once a $5,000 'principal only' payment the servicer left unapplied is credited, a notice of error for that payment, and a notice that the parent's insurance ended at 78% on 1 August while premiums kept coming; a request on the parent's loan after a 52-day late payment, a closed credit line, a paid-off condo loan, a $399 removal service, a look-alike phishing address and a second payment left alone; run 1: 3 of 3, every card exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.7 min; filmed.

- `feat(playbooks): the site's stormwater inspections and corrective actions kept on the permit's schedule across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 16: EPA's 2022 Construction General Permit as the site's SWPPP states it, read against the rain gauge, the inspection reports and the dated photos; a storm inspection assigned for the 0.34 inches that fell overnight, three corrective actions opened (a fence down with sediment off site, a stockpile never covered, the third sediment removal at the same fence) and two closed on the dates the photos show; a weekend storm already inspected, two drizzles never 0.25 inches within 24 hours, a 0.11-inch storm, three routine maintenance findings, one already opened, a fence with posts and no fabric and one already closed left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, 4 exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.3 min; filmed. $5,000 a year, mostly minutes: each act is weighted at $250. B2's first half is complete.

- `feat(playbooks): city inspections booked in sequence on two cities' portals, on the first day each can happen, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 15: each city's adoption of IBC section 110 read against the field status and the crew calendar; a rough electrical re-inspection after its corrections, a rough mechanical and a footing before Thursday's pour booked for Wednesday (Monday a city holiday, Tuesday the superintendent away), and two under-slab trades and a canopy's electrical final in the next city for Monday; framing before every rough trade, insulation not installed, a gas test already booked, a foundation and under-slab plumbing not ready, a final, a slab pre-pour before its trades, a guardrail re-inspection before the fix and a foundation already passed left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, every date exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.5 min; filmed. $8,000 a year, mostly minutes: each booking is weighted at $400.

B1 is complete.

- `feat(playbooks): a federal grant drawn down against what was spent and the quarter's SF-425 filed across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 10: each ledger entry and payroll line read against the award's terms, the Uniform Guidance's cost principles and the timesheets, five allowable paid costs drawn at their allowable share and the SF-425 filed with cumulative cash-basis expenditures; a pre-award license, tablets already drawn, wine, a gala, lobbying, an IRS penalty, another award's rent, an unpaid invoice and a fundraiser's payroll left alone; run 1: 6 of 6, $31,492.60, every act exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.4 min; filmed. $2,500 a quarter, mostly minutes.

- `feat(playbooks): every new hire screened for the Work Opportunity Tax Credit across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 7: Form 8850 answers, signing days and start dates read across the tracker, payroll and the mail, each hire under the target group worth most inside the 28 days; a late form, the owner's nephew, a rehire, a form signed after the offer, a 47-year-old in a SNAP household, a hire in no group, a veteran out of work three weeks, a form already in and a no-show left alone; the world dated December 2025, before the credit lapsed for 2026 hires; run 1: 5 of 5, $19,200, every form exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.0 min; filmed.

- `feat(playbooks): data brokers told to delete and checked across the state's platform and two people-search sites, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 6: the Delete Act's statuses read against their 45 days, a former name added, her own listings removed on each site's form, a complaint only for the broker past its deadline; namesakes, her mother, her adult son, a lawful opt-out, an exemption and brokers inside their time left alone; two runs, the world fixed once (the request already listed her old address); run 2: 5 of 5, 10 of 10 traps avoided, 0 false, 1.9 min; filmed. $0 a year, mostly minutes: its items are weighted at a removal service's price.

- `feat(playbooks): a household's Medicaid renewal answered across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 5: stale wage and benefit lines corrected to current monthly income under 42 CFR 435.603 (taxable wages after a pre-tax 401(k), a job and benefits that ended), a mother with her own return taken off the household, only the proof asked for; child support, a loan, a refund, a teenager's pay, the mother's Social Security and an old stub left out; run 1: 8 of 8, every correction exact, 8 of 8 traps avoided, 0 false, 2.8 min; filmed.

- `feat(playbooks): a family's delayed flights claimed under EU 261, across four portals, benched to exceeds` — ADR 0057 row 1: arrival times in the mail, causes on each airline's status page, scope, notice, extraordinary circumstances and free passengers as traps, a settlement voucher left alone; run 1: 4 of 4, $5,445 (4,950 euros), every claim exact, 9 of 9 traps avoided, 0 false, 3.1 min; filmed.

- `fix(playbooks): evidence --verify catches a narration that drifted from its record` — ADR 0055's flaw: a rescore keeps `run_at`, so the template is filled again and compared with the committed text; `narration.sha256` is stated to be the WAV's.

- `chore(playbooks): the nine shipped playbooks filmed` — `evidence --all` on Kokoro af_heart: films of 63.5 to 70.1 s, one H.264 and one AAC stream each, kept in the gitignored `evidence/`; the nine `evidence.json` indexes (schema 1, current against their bench) and thumbnails of 74 to 99.9 KB committed.

- `feat(playbooks): a benched playbook is filmed — the evidence verb narrates it, speaks it with Kokoro, captures the layer and muxes the film` — ADR 0055; `evidence <id>|--all [--dry] [--verify]`, a deterministic 45–90 s narration from playbook.json and bench.json, the media in a gitignored `evidence/`, `evidence.json` (schema 1) and a thumbnail under 100 KB committed beside the playbook.

- `feat(playbooks): a filmed run shows on its tile and opens as a part of the layer` — ADR 0055; `parseEvidence` reads `evidence.json` and `thumb.jpg` through eager globs into `Playbook.evidence`; the tile carries the still and the length, *The run, filmed* plays the film where the dev server serves `evidence/` (byte ranges) and shows the still elsewhere, with the narration and current or stale against the bench; a `shipped:<id>` fixture per shipped playbook.

- `fix(hirelane): the dossier offers exactly the acts the server actions accept, from one module both read.` DESIGN-LAW 7.7, ADR 0107 (scan-sweep challenge, architecture-challenger): the gate panel listed only `status = 'open'` slots, so a candidate whose slots Propose slots had just held was offered none of them; `lib/acts.ts` (`actsFor`, its predicates and `REFUSAL`) is now what the actions guard with, the dossier draws from, and the agent's dossier read and `read_view` carry. Tests: test/acts.test.ts (a parity matrix of every seeded applicant x six acts against the real actions), two guards in test/actions.test.ts.

- `feat(connectors): credential health is a verdict plus an attempt, written by every door of the vault.` README 4, ADR 0108 amending 0021 and 0062 (scan-sweep challenge, architecture-challenger): `connectors/health.py` classifies what the probe, a brokered call or a refresh learnt into a verdict or an attempt that reached none; an unreachable provider, a 429 or a 5xx no longer marks a credential broken, the probe refreshes a stale OAuth token first, a brokered 401 is needs_reauth and broken with its time, a failed refresh is an attempt on the record, the 0062 reset sentence is a `notice` a probe keeps, and Accounts reads "Verified N ago; could not check since M ago" as a warning.

- `fix(harness): a CLI round ends in a typed exit, and a gone or poisoned thread is reseeded once.` README 3.1, ADR 0109 amending 0007 and 0031 (scan-sweep challenge, architecture-challenger): each `CliRequest` carries an exit handle the transport reports to after its last line (`Transport.run` still yields lines only, so the adopt legs are untouched); the dialect classifies the exit, the result record and the stderr tail into gone, poisoned, auth, limit, transient or unknown from a dated table, each with a fixed sentence and never the CLI's own text; a non-zero exit or a stream with no result record is a failure, not an empty success; a resumed round that is gone or poisoned drops its session id and is asked again once, unresumed, in the same turn. Tests: `tests/harness/test_cli_exit.py`, red then green.

- `feat(voice): a heard utterance ends in a typed outcome that crosses the wire, and silence reaches no engine.` README 3.2, ADR 0110 amending 0019 (scan-sweep challenge, architecture-challenger): the final `voice.transcript` is sent after `recognise` and carries `outcome` (`message`, `answer`, `stop`, `too_short`, `silent`, `no_words`, one tuple in `contracts/channel.py` and `lib/events.ts`) and `peak_dbfs`; `BufferedTranscriber` measures the peak and stops a capture below -55 dBFS before whisper or the provider (no request, no row); the voice store leaves `thinking` on every outcome but `message` and `answer`, with its own sentence. Tests: test_channel (round trip, old shape, TS parity), test_voice_openai, test_voice_gateway, test_commands, voice.test.ts.

- `feat(hirelane): every reversible act on the board offers its undo, on the toast and in the dossier's History band.` DESIGN-LAW 7, ADR 0111 (scan-sweep challenge, ux-elevation): the board said each AUTO act writes an undo, but no surface called `undoAction`; the four AUTO writes now return `undoId`, the kit Toast takes an optional action (ledgerbox and tidycrm unchanged), the dossier lists a person's acts newest first (8 of N) with Undo or the reason there is none, and the decided-move refusal no longer names an Undo the log would refuse. Tests: test/history.test.ts (toast action, History order and undone state, irreversible reason, the 8-of-12 bound), test/actions.test.ts (undoId on move and on note/score/propose, none on the gated three, the refusal's remedy).

- `feat(demo-kit): the rig's pointer captures when a press becomes a drag, and eats the click after it.` kit-camera-contract §2, ADR 0112 (scan-sweep challenge): `useCameraRig` captured on every press, so a DOM control in a camera scene lost its mouse click (KIT-GAPS R5-A1, four reports). The pointer is now the pure machine `zoom/gesture.ts`: a click until 4 screen px, capture at that threshold, the trailing click of a drag swallowed once (cleared by the next press or when the up's task ends), and every exit releases. Atlas's lanes nodes drop `holdPointer`, so a pan can start on one. `test/gesture.test.ts` pins each case.

- `feat(voice): a spoken host call reads its timeout and is held as a typed one is, from one executor both stores drive.` README 3.2, ADR 0113 amending 0101 (scan-sweep challenge, architecture-challenger): `stores/voice.ts` ran host calls through its own copy of the loop with no timeout reading, no hold and no unsure tools in `gated_tools`, so a timed-out spoken send read as a failure and a tool the panel held ran on the next spoken turn; the rules moved into `lib/execute.ts` (run.ts re-exports the old names), voice drives them, and a spoken answer to a card lifts its hold. Tests: lib/execute.test.ts, voice.test.ts (seven cases, six red before); run.test.ts unchanged.

- `feat(voice): her spoken reply passes one speech door: no markup, whole sentences, a 360-character budget.` README 3.2, ADR 0114 amending 0019 (scan-sweep challenge, ux-elevation): with no `TTS:` line (no prompt teaches one) the visible reply was spoken as written, markdown and URLs included, and cut at character 1,200 mid-word; the new pure `channels/voice/speech.py` makes it speakable (a link is its label, a bare URL its host, a fence one 'code on screen' sentence), owns the sentence rules `kokoro.split_sentences` now uses (no cut at 3.5, Dr. or e.g.), and the fallback is spoken as whole sentences up to `SPOKEN_BUDGET` with `(showing N of M)` counted in sentences. Tests: `tests/channels/test_speech_text.py` (eight cases, six red before).

- `feat(harness, daemon, desktop): a running turn can be stopped, and the 25-minute wall is the turn's, not a line's.` README 3.2 and 3.5, ADR 0115 amending 0011 (scan-sweep challenge, ux-elevation): `TURN_TIMEOUT_S` bounded one `readline`, so a CLI writing a line every few minutes never stopped and eight rounds could each wait 25 minutes while the writer lock held every approval and new tab. Each round now runs inside what is left of the turn's wall, and a request carries that remainder. `POST /run/stop {origin}` (token-checked, no writer lock) cancels the running turn's step, so the turn writes one `cancelled` row and ends in `turn.error`. The panel's Stop aborts the request, ends the continuation loop, and carries any page result she had not read with the next message. A hang-up through the lane now writes its row too.

- `fix(harness): the real spawn reports how its process ended, so a live CLI round is classified as ADR 0109 says.` README 3.2, ADR 0109 (scan-sweep challenge, coordinator fix to harness-engines/A): `SubprocessTransport` never reported to the request's exit handle, so every real claude or codex run read as exit 0 and the typed-exit classes only ever fired against the scripted transport. It now reads the stderr tail beside stdout and reports the exit code and tail after the last line; tests/harness/test_subprocess_exit.py drives a real child process.

- `feat(desktop): a connector card names the account it acts as, and says when the record will refuse it.` README 3.1, ADR 0116 amending 0026 (scan-sweep challenge, ux-elevation): a Gmail card read `In connector:gmail`; it now reads `In Gmail - a connector, not a page` and `Acts as mira@example.com through Gmail.`, or names the one cause (a lapsed grant, switched off, writes off) and that approving will be refused, with `Open Main`. Approve and Decline never read it. Her window reads `/connectors` once per connector card that arrives; a successful act emits `connectors:changed`, replacing the dead `store:changed` branch; `isWrite` defers to `gate.js` `classify`. Tests: connectors model and store, companion model and views (cases 1-7).
- `feat(connectors): a Gmail grant that needs reconnecting is one Reconnect press, reusing the client the vault already sealed.` README 4, ADR 0117 (scan-sweep challenge, connector-service/B): `connect_oauth(reuse_client=True)` unseals the pair from the last admission and a pasted pair still wins; `Vault.view` says `client_kept`, a yes or a no; a kept-client flow leaves its authorize URL out of every answer, so no client value crosses the route; a failed or cancelled reconnect seals and destroys nothing, and ADR 0062's switch reset is unchanged. Accounts shows one Reconnect with "Use a different client" behind it. Tests: test_grant cases 0, 1, 3, 4; test_routes cases 2 and 5 (guard); model, store and interact case 6 and 7.
- `fix(hirelane): the dossier's staged rise reads the spacing step off the cascade instead of typing a copy of it.` DESIGN-LAW §1 (scan-sweep stabilize, registry-conformance, design-tokens/cross-language-token-parity): `components/board/motion.ts` typed `RISE = 8` under a comment naming `--hl-space-2`; it now reads `--bd-s2` through `useTokens` with the durations, so a rebased spacing scale moves the dossier's entrance with every gap. Test: test/motion.test.ts "the staged rise is the spacing scale's own step".
- `fix(hirelane): the contrast check scores the slide's top stop by the token the slide paints, not by an alpha typed beside it.` DESIGN-LAW §4.2 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor, design-tokens/cross-language-token-parity): the carousel card's gradient stops are `--bd-slide-top` / `--bd-slide-foot`, `l1-carousel.css` paints with them and `design/check-contrast.mjs` composites the top stop by name instead of a typed `alpha: 0.08`. Test: test/contrast.test.ts.
- `fix(connectors): a connector write that timed out or was answered 5xx is outcome_unknown, not a failure.` README 4, ADR 0101 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler): the vault's transport raises `NoAnswer` for a timeout or a dropped connection after the request went out; `Service.call` answers such a write, or one whose last request got a 5xx, with `outcome_unknown` and "the write may have gone through"; reads and 4xx writes are unchanged. Tests: test_service no-answer, never-sent and 5xx cases.
- `fix(hirelane): the slide's median head drops a branch that could never run.` (scan-sweep stabilize, code-optimizer): `carousel/Slide.tsx` printed `medians.size === 0 ? 0 : column.scored` inside a branch that `comparable` (which requires `medians.size > 0`) already guards; it prints `column.scored`. Proof: `git grep medians.size` finds only the guard and the dead arm.
- `fix(harness): no CLI child inherits a billing key, so OPENAI_API_KEY no longer reaches codex.` README 3.1, ADR 0007 (scan-sweep stabilize, security-auditor, agent-cli-transport/subscription-auth-selection): `child_env` stripped only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; it now strips every CLI engine's billing switch (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `CODEX_API_KEY`) from every child, in any case, from one dated `ENGINE_KEYS` table pinned to `ENGINES`. A real child spawned through the door saw 5 of the 6 before and sees none after. `NEBIUS_API_KEY` still passes: the Proving Ground's own child needs it through this door.
- `fix(hirelane): the masthead and crumb totals obey the arguable-only filter, from the one filter module the columns draw with.` DESIGN-LAW §8 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `Board.tsx` totalled by role only while `Columns.tsx` also dropped the unflagged, so "arguable only" left the headline and crumb reading the whole pipeline over columns summing to six. Both read `components/board/columns/filter.ts` (`groupsAt`, `boardTotals`). Test: test/filter.test.ts.
- `fix(harness): the Cedar rendering names every input the policy reads, and a parity test keeps it so.` README 3.3, 3.4 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `Policy.to_cedar` had drifted from `Policy.authorize` in three places - no clause for the per-turn `context.disabled_origins` (ADR 0011), no first-sight exclusion from `context.open_apps`, and a pin clause that compared the session's page with every app's pin, forbidding every cross-tab proposal ADR 0106 permits. A test now drives every rule branch through a context that records what was read and asks the Cedar text for each input: 3 missing before, 0 after.
- `fix(connectors): a garbled or cut-short provider answer fails the call instead of ending the turn.` README 4 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler): `http.client`'s `BadStatusLine` and `IncompleteRead` are not `OSError`s and escaped the vault's transport to the round loop's catch-all; they are now `NoAnswer`. Test: test_vault garbled-answer cases.
- `fix(hirelane): an armed irreversible act starts on Cancel, so a held or doubled Enter cannot record an offer.` DESIGN-LAW §7 (scan-sweep stabilize, accessibility-checker, modal-stack/destructive-confirmation): `dossier/Gate.tsx` auto-focused the offer's fire button when it armed, and the reject and schedule arms dropped focus to the document; all three now start on their own Cancel. Test: test/gate.test.ts.
- `fix(harness): a CLI's own sign-in check decides logged_in, so a stale credential file no longer reads as signed in.` README 3.1, ADR 0118 amends ADR 0007 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler, agent-cli-transport/availability-probe): `probe` runs `claude auth status --json` or `codex login status` (zero tokens; verified 2026-10-16 on 2.1.301 and 0.160.0) at or above the verified version, in the spawn's own environment with no stdin, and reads only the verdict; below that floor the credential-file guess stands, and an unreadable check is unknown, never yes. A signed-out CLI with its file still on disk read `found` before and reads `not_logged_in` after; about +0.5 s per CLI per probe.
- `fix(hirelane): Escape in the note field cancels the draft without also closing the candidate's dossier.` Formula §1 rule 5 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter, modal-stack/dismissal-semantics): the note input in `dossier/Acts.tsx` closed the draft on Escape but left the key un-prevented, so it bubbled to the dossier's `useOverlayEscape` and closed the candidate too; it now calls `preventDefault()`. Test: test/note.test.ts.
- `fix(voice): the newest-cards test reads the session of the one socket it opens, so a slow handler thread no longer hands it two.` ADR 0073 (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): the test asked for an unused `client` socket whose session the daemon's handler thread can build after `connect` returns, so under load the patched `VoiceSession.__init__` saw two sessions. It drops the fixture and reads only this daemon's sessions. Before: 3 of 20 failed under a 16-process CPU burn, and 1 of 1 with a 0.3 s handler delay probe; after: 0 of 32 under the burn, 1 of 1 green with the probe.
- `fix(voice): a broken engine verdict is asked again after a minute, so a repaired engine is heard without restarting the daemon.` README 3.1, ADR 0028 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler, voice-io/engine-abstraction): `home.dry_check` kept a broken verdict per executable size and mtime forever, so a DLL put back beside the engine, or a first run a virus scan held past the timeout, read as broken until restart. A ready verdict is still kept per file version; a broken one stands for `BROKEN_RECHECK_S` (60 s). Test: test_voice_engines dry-check cases.
- `fix(connectors): the writes switch blocks a connector tool whose spec leaves reversible unset, as the catalog gates it.` README 4, ADR 0116 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `service.is_write` read `reversible is False`, the catalog and the panel read anything not declared reversible as a write; it is now `default_class() is GATED`, one rule. Test: test_service reversible-unset case.
- `fix(hirelane): the foot's AUTO band says how many tools write an undo and how many only read, instead of claiming all thirteen do.` DESIGN-LAW §7.4, §8 (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): "Auto — reversible, and each writes an undo" headed 13 AUTO tools, 9 of which only read or move the view; `components/board/register.ts` derives `AUTO_BAND` from the manifest rows and `shell/Foot.tsx` prints it. Test: test/register.test.ts.
- `fix(lane): a switch change whose turn failed still gives the next frame the whole picture.` README 3.2 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter): `FrameBuilder` recorded a turn's switched-off origins when it built the frame, but the host-state baseline only when a turn finished; after a switch change whose turn failed, the next turn under the same switch got a delta ("nothing moved") against what the old CLI session was shown, sent to a new session that had been shown nothing. The switch is now committed by `remember`, like the host state.
- `fix(voice): a bug in an install run or a Kokoro render thread leaves its traceback in the log, not only its type name.` README 3.1, ADR 0028 (scan-sweep stabilize, observability-auditor): `Installer._run` reported a bug as "install failed (RuntimeError)" and swallowed a raising studio refresh; `kokoro._Render` turned a bug into a VoiceBackendError the speaker reads as an engine refusal and does not log. Both log the traceback once now; what the person sees is unchanged and an engine's own refusal still logs nothing. Tests: test_voice_install and test_voice_engines bug cases.
- `fix(hirelane): Enter on the carousel rail opens the card under the loupe, as the rail's own header promises.` (scan-sweep stabilize, accessibility-checker): the rail takes focus when a group opens but its handler knew only the arrows; Enter and Space on the rail now open the centre card, a key aimed at a slide button stays that button's, and the hint says so. Test: test/rail.test.ts.
- `fix(harness): a model call that raises is named by its type alone, so a refused key never reaches a turn's error.` AGENTS.md "Never log a secret" (scan-sweep stabilize, security-auditor): `ApiHarness` put a raising `ModelFn`'s message in the `TurnError` detail, unlike the round loop's own catch-all, and `http.client` raises with the `Authorization` header in its message for a `NEBIUS_API_KEY` with a line break in it, before any socket opens. The detail now names the exception type only; reproduced offline red before, green after.
- `fix(connectors): a spec whose tools the catalog would refuse is refused at load, not after a credential is sealed.` README 4, ADR 0021 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `parse_spec` now applies `HostManifest.validate` to its tools; before, a tool with no `reversible` loaded, was sealed for at connect, and then raised `CatalogError` out of the re-merge (a 500, and a daemon that would not start). Tests: test_spec_and_oauth three refusal cases.
- `fix(voice): a TTS line the speech door lengthens is held to its cap again, so Kokoro never refuses it whole.` README 3.2, ADR 0114 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter, voice-io/speech-ready-text): the parser cuts the `TTS:` line to 1200 before the door, but a bare URL spoken as "a link to" its host is longer than a short URL, so a line at the cap left the door at up to 1372 characters and Kokoro (limit 1264) refused it whole. `spoken_line` now holds the door's output to `TTS_CAP`, cut by whole sentences and announced like the text fallback; kokoro's `TEXT_CAP` is pinned to be at least the cap. Test: test_speech_text case 5b.
- `fix(hirelane): the carousel arrows and the gated buttons ease every property their hover changes, like their sibling controls.` DESIGN-LAW §1.3 (scan-sweep stabilize, ui-perfectionist): `.bd-arrow` had no transition beside the back pill's identical hover, and the GATED buttons' edge snapped while `.bd-btn` eased only its fill; both now ease on their siblings' tokens. Test: test/hover.test.ts (every :hover rule against its rest-state transition).
- `fix(lane): an approved card whose tool left the catalog is ledgered like any refused replay.` README 3.2 step 6 (scan-sweep stabilize, observability-auditor): of the two ways an approved card is refused, a gate refusal at the replay wrote an error row and the action missing from the catalog (its page reloaded without it) wrote none and reported the card as not approved. Both now write the `rounds = 0` row with their reason.
- `fix(voice): the install phases, the running set and the preview bound are pinned to the desktop's copies of them.` README 3.1, ADR 0028 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `install.py`'s `InstallPhase` and `INSTALL_STATES`, `RUNNING` and `kokoro.TEXT_CAP` were mirrored by hand in `apps/desktop/src/lib/voice-setup.ts` (`InstallPhase`, `ACTIVE_INSTALL`, which the desktop polls on, and `PREVIEW_MAX_CHARS`) with no parity test. test_voice_install now reads voice-setup.ts as test_channel reads events.ts; three seeded drifts go red.
- `fix(voice): the WebSocket module stops naming an `athena voice` client that does not exist.` README 3.5, ADR 0019 (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): `ws.py` said its client half exists so `athena voice` can be a shell-free client; the CLI has no voice command and `connect`'s only callers are tests, which is what it now says.
- `fix(connectors): an OAuth admission takes the daemon's writer lock, so its catalog re-merge never lands beside a running turn.` README 4, ADR 0021 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter): `connect_oauth(guard=daemon.writing)`; the flow thread exchanges the code unguarded, then admits, seals and re-merges under the lock every other connector act takes. Test: test_routes writer-lock case.
- `fix(harness): a native tool call whose arguments do not parse meets the grammar's repairs, and never runs with none.` README 3.2, ADR 0031 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): Token Factory's adapter turned native tool-call arguments it could not parse into `params = {}`, so a trailing comma lost every argument and an unreadable call ran with none; the `OP:` grammar would have repaired the first and refused the second. Unreadable arguments now ride as written into the `OP:` line, through the same seven repairs or a told `parse_error`.
- `fix(connectors): a consent flow cancelled while its code is exchanged seals nothing and stays failed.` README 4, ADR 0021 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter): the vault asks `OAuthFlow.claim()` under its lock before sealing, and a cancel shares the flow's phase lock, so a replaced or stopped flow keeps nothing; the TTL fails only an unanswered consent page. Test: test_grant cancelled-while-exchanged case.
- `fix(harness): a CLI that exits before reading its input still reports its exit, not a broken pipe.` ADR 0109 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler, subprocess-lifecycle/termination-and-reaping): the real spawn wrote stdin before its reaping `try`, so a CLI that refused at startup (a removed flag, not signed in) with a frame larger than the pipe buffer raised `BrokenPipeError` out of the transport, unclassified and unreaped. The write is inside the `try` now and a closed pipe is skipped; a real child exiting 2 unread reports exit 2 and its stderr tail.
- `fix(harness): an engine probe opens no console window, like a turn's spawn.` (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor, subprocess-lifecycle/spawn-contract): the turn's spawn passes `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` because the daemon is a windowless sidecar, but `engines.probe` started `--version` and the sign-in check without it, so each probe flashed a console per spawn. Both probe spawns and the turn's now use one `transports.NO_WINDOW`; red before (`[None, None]`), green after.
- `fix(connectors): the consent callback page says what happened, not "connected" before the grant is admitted.` README 4 (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): the loopback page waits up to 10 s for the exchange and answers connected, could not finish connecting (with the vault's sentence), or still finishing; a declined consent no longer reads "This page is not for this flow." Tests: test_spec_and_oauth three browser-page cases.
- `fix(connectors): a settings switch that is not JSON true or false is refused, so "false" can no longer turn writes on.` README 4 (scan-sweep stabilize, security-auditor): the settings route coerced switches with `bool()`; `enabled` and `writes_enabled` must now be booleans, read before anything is applied, else a 409 that changes nothing. Test: test_routes switch cases.
- `fix(connectors): a disconnect destroys every sealed value even when the seal can no longer be read.` README 4 (scan-sweep stabilize, error-handler): an unreadable token (a locked keychain) raised out of `Vault.disconnect` before anything was destroyed; it now means nothing to revoke, and the drop and the record go ahead. Test: test_seal unreadable-seal disconnect.
- `fix(connectors): a pasted token answered 401 on a real call is recorded as broken, as the probe already reads it.` README 4, ADR 0108 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `Vault.request` writes a broken verdict for a token connector's 401 (the record stays connected, the answer is returned as before); a 403 is not a verdict. Test: test_health case 5 for a pasted token.
- `fix(connectors): each provider dispatches through a handler table that a parity test holds to its spec's tools.` README 4, ADR 0021 (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): `gmail.TOOLS` and `notion.TOOLS` replace the per-name if-chains (bodies unchanged); a test derived from the builtin specs and the providers package pins handlers to tools both ways, seeded red by dropping one. Tests: test_providers parity and no-handler cases.
- `fix(connectors): the connector read cap is pinned to the catalog's own, which it copies.` README 2 (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): `service.READ_CAP` copies `core.catalog.READ_CAP` (an import would cycle); a test now holds them equal, seeded red at 1500. Test: test_service read-cap case.
- `fix(atlas): every ADR the model lists is checked against the file in docs/adr it names, its number and its H1.` examples/atlas data/types.ts content rule (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): the model test read only the hand-kept ADRS list; `test/data.test.ts` now reads docs/adr for each entry's file, number and H1, seeded red on a retitled copy. Test: data.test ADR case.
- `fix(desktop): the shell's command names are one checked set across build.rs, lib.rs, the capabilities and the calls.` README 3.5 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `lib/commands.test.ts` reads the app manifest, `generate_handler!`, every capability's grants and every `call` site from their own files and holds them equal both ways, with `athena_snap_to` a named idle loan; seeded red by dropping one handler. Test: commands.test.ts.
- `fix(atlas): set_turn refuses a fractional stop instead of crashing the story bar.` DESIGN.md R7 (scan-sweep stabilize, bounty-hunter): the stop check moved into a pure `askStop` in `archify-lanes/turn.ts` and requires a whole number; 2.5 used to be stored as 1.5 and the story bar threw on an undefined beat. Test: lanes.poses set_turn whole-stop case.
- `fix(atlas): set_turn tell=false stops the story clock, as the TURN toggle and the view switch do.` DESIGN.md R7 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): the tool's state change is a pure `turnAfter` in `archify-lanes/turn.ts` and LanesTools now sets `playing` too; before, its answer said stopped while the clock kept stepping a hidden story. Test: lanes.poses set_turn tell=false case.
- `fix(atlas): the model draws the window that is built - one scene with depths and her own window - not the deleted module bar.` README 3.5, ADR 0026, 0087, 0095 (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): concept D-3, system S-02, lib.rs and four edges still described the module bar ADR 0095 deleted, and three entries cited files no longer in the tree; ids unchanged, six ADRs added. Test: data.test citation cases, red on 3 missing paths.
- `fix(atlas): a lane node's accessible name says which lane owns it and which phase it happens in.` archify-lanes position thesis (scan-sweep stabilize, accessibility-checker): `nodeName` in `archify-lanes/workflow.ts` adds "In lane …, phase …" to the aria-label; the lane and phase chrome is aria-hidden, so 0 of 12 names said where a node stands. Test: lanes.model accessible-name case.
- `fix(atlas): a lane node's tag and a fine run label fade in at L1 as they fade out at L0.` DESIGN.md §7 box-then-ink (scan-sweep stabilize, ui-perfectionist): the opacity transition moved from the `[data-band="0"]` rule onto `.al-node-tag` / `.al-run-label`, so entering L1 no longer pops the detail in. Test: new `test/lanes.css.test.ts` detail-tier case.
- `fix(atlas): the lint gate reads data/, the model every surface and tool reads, instead of ignoring it.` AGENTS.md gate (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): `eslint.config.mjs` ignored `data/**` since the app's first commit; the seven files now lint clean, and a seeded `any` there turns lint red. Test: the lint gate itself, seeded.
- `fix(companion): a spent approval has its own plain sentence, and the reasons the test walks are the gate's own list.` README 3.1, ADR 0038 (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): `plainFailure` and `plainReason` say an `approval_spent` approval was already used once instead of the catch-all; `plain.test.ts` reads `REFUSAL_REASONS` from gate.js and fails when a gate reason has no sentence. Test: plain.test.ts.
- `fix(atlas): a run and the story trail ease their opacity when the story steps, as the node beside them does.` DESIGN.md §7 (scan-sweep stabilize, ui-perfectionist): the run's opacity transition sat on `.al-run-line` while `data-dim` dims `.al-run`, and the trail had none; `.al-run`, `.al-trail-line` and `.al-trail-mark` now ease on `--al-dur-lens`. Test: lanes.css story-dim case.
- `fix(atlas): the token gate checks the token file's own alias layer for a mistyped --at-* token.` examples/atlas DESIGN.md 9 (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): `design/check-tokens.mjs` skipped `tokens.css` whole, so a typo in its `--dk-*` alias layer passed lint; only the raw-literal check skips it now. Test: check-tokens.test (new), seeded red.
- `fix(companion): her tool rows are built from the manifest's own list, so a page's tabs_read is dropped there too.` README 3.4, ADR 0065 and 0105 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `toolRows` maps `withHands` and reads the tier off the hand marker instead of re-merging with its own filter, which showed a page's `tabs_read` as a tier-1 tool and hid the shell's hand. Test: companion/tools.test.ts.
- `fix(atlas): the lane sheet's TURN, chapter, home and theme controls say their state and purpose, not only in colour.` WCAG 4.1.2 (scan-sweep stabilize, accessibility-checker): TURN carries `aria-pressed`, the current chapter `aria-current="step"`, and home and theme an action name that keeps the visible text. Test: new `test/lanes.chrome.test.ts`.
- `fix(atlas): the token gate skips data, design and test only at the app root, not every folder of that name.` examples/atlas DESIGN.md 9 (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): the skip list matched a folder name at any depth, so a component folder called `data` would have gone unread; node_modules and .next stay skipped everywhere. Test: check-tokens.test nested-folder case, seeded red.
- `fix(bridge): the inject tests wait for each reply instead of sleeping a fixed time, so a busy machine no longer fails them.` protocol.md Timeouts (scan-sweep stabilize, test-strategist): eleven tests slept 5-100 ms then asserted the reply; they now poll for it (3 s ceiling on a hang). Under a 72-thread CPU burner: 4 of 30 runs failed before, 0 of 60 after.
- `fix(atlas): the page's description names the lane diagram it draws, not the deleted round-5 sheet.` examples/atlas README (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): the metadata description still read "nineteen systems and sixty-eight modules on one sheet, rows for layers and columns for kinds"; it now names four lanes, the columns and twelve nodes. Test: none (probe, 2 stale claims to 0).
- `fix(atlas): the lane sheet's zoom buttons say when the camera is at its bound instead of answering a press with nothing.` archify-lanes ZOOM_BOUNDS (scan-sweep stabilize, state-coverage): a pure `zoomRoom` in `archify-lanes/poses.ts`; the Toolbar marks the bound button `aria-disabled` (focus kept) with a guarded handler, dimmed in lanes.css. Tests: lanes.chrome zoom case, lanes.poses zoom-room case.
- `fix(bridge): a call for a tool the page never registered answers unknown_ref, and an unknown request validator_failed, apart from a tool that threw.` README 3.3, ADR 0009 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): the three failures shared one uncoded shape; the two refusals now carry existing vocabulary members (as the catalog and the hands script already do), a handler fault none. Test: inject.test.js reason case, seeded red.
- `fix(atlas): the drawn face loads the four weights the stylesheets use, not a fifth no rule asks for.` examples/atlas DESIGN.md 3 (scan-sweep stabilize, code-optimizer): Chivo was loaded at 900, which no stylesheet, inline style or kit rule selects; it is dropped (weights declared 5 to 4, all used). Test: none (probe).
- `fix(atlas): the lane legend reads its role words and label advance from workflow.ts and routing.ts instead of re-typing them.` archify-lanes one-footprint rule (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `ROLE_LEGEND` derives from `ROLES`/`ROLE_LABEL`, the legend measures with `LABEL_ADVANCE`, and that is `ADVANCE * TIERS.sublabel.base`; copies of the words 2 -> 1, of the advance 3 -> 1. Test: lanes.model legend-owner case.
- `fix(atlas): the model's and the constants' header comments name the test file, the system count and the switchers that exist.` AGENTS.md module citations (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): data/index.ts named a `test/lens.test.ts` that does not exist, systems.ts said eighteen systems over nineteen, and lib/constants.ts denied the variant switcher round 7 added. Test: none (probe, 4 stale claims to 0).
- `fix(desktop): the panel's channel events are held to channel.py field for field, family for family.` README 3.2 (scan-sweep stabilize, parity-auditor): `lib/events.test.ts` reads `contracts/channel.py` and pins the kinds, `FAMILIES`, every event's fields and which may be null; seeded red by dropping `TurnSummary.rounds`. Test: events.test.ts.
- `fix(atlas): the lanes folder drops eighteen exports the deleted archify sheet left behind with no consumer.` DESIGN.md R6.3 (scan-sweep stabilize, code-optimizer): `git grep -nw` found only the definition for each (kinds.ts nine, workflow.ts four, poses.ts three, `stopAt`, `planKey`); about 100 lines gone, held by typecheck and the full atlas test run.
- `fix(bridge): a call's input is checked against the tool's own inputSchema before the page runs it.` README 3.3 (scan-sweep stabilize, security-auditor): inject.js ran any input; it now checks the gate's subset at dispatch against the schema the tool publishes now and answers validator_failed, never stricter than check_schema. Test: inject.test.js, fifteen inputs pinned to the gate's verdicts, seeded red.
- `fix(atlas): the lanes modules' headers stop citing a deleted test, a frame nobody draws and a world ten units too wide.` AGENTS.md cite-your-section (scan-sweep stabilize, copy-auditor): seven header claims in `archify-lanes/` corrected (kinds, script, Scene, routing, lanes.css). Test: lanes.model cited-tests-exist case, seeded red with the old citation.
